# SHE STOLE MY FACE — EP01

## S#1. Westwood Cemetery — Mother's Grave · Late Afternoon

[VISUAL/ACTION]
A private cemetery on a slope above the city. Manicured grass, a single marble headstone reading HELENA STERLING — 1968-2018. LENA STERLING — late-20s, ink-black hair pinned back, navy silk-wool tailored suit — kneels and sets a small bouquet against the stone. Her hand goes to the gold-diamond pendant at her throat. She turns it once between two fingers. Pulls it back into place.

[KEY CAMERA]
- ECU Lena's fingers on the pendant, the inscription side hidden against her skin
- MCU Lena kneeling, eyes on the engraved name
- OTS over the headstone to her face
- Wide of the empty cemetery, one car waiting on the access road

[DIALOGUE]
LENA
Mom. The dress fitting was today. I almost cried — and you know I don't do that. I think you would've liked it. Tomorrow night Victoria's announcing it in front of half the city. So this is me telling you first.

She breathes out, smooths the grass with her palm.

LENA (CONT'D)
I'm wearing your necklace. Of course I'm wearing your necklace. I'll bring it back to you the morning after.

[VO]
LENA (V.O.)
I keep telling myself the worst thing already happened. I'm thirty. My mom's been gone eight years. Whatever else comes, it can't be worse than that.

## S#2. Cross Manor — Ballroom (Rehearsal) · Early Evening

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Cross Manor — a glass-and-stone modern estate. Caterers and a string quartet rehearsing. VICTORIA CROSS — late-50s, silver-streaked dark hair in a French twist, charcoal Chanel-cut tweed — moves through the staff with a clipboard. ETHAN CROSS — early-30s, charcoal three-piece — stands at a microphone testing levels. Lena enters, still in the navy suit, mother's necklace at her throat.

[KEY CAMERA]
- MCU Victoria intercepting Lena at the doorway, a once-over from collar to shoes
- Two-shot Ethan crossing the floor toward Lena, hands in his pockets
- ECU Victoria's eyes catching on a faint shadow under Lena's jawline
- CU Lena's polite smile holding

[DIALOGUE]
VICTORIA
Lena, sweetheart. Stand in the light a moment.

She tips Lena's chin with one finger. Studies her.

VICTORIA (CONT'D)
There's a hollow under your jaw. The camera will eat it tomorrow. I made you an appointment at Dr. Brandt's clinic — six o'clock tonight. Just a touch. The press line starts at eight in the morning.

LENA
Victoria, I'm fine. I had four hours of sleep, that's all.

VICTORIA
You're the future face of this family in twelve hours. You'll go.

Ethan reaches them. He sets his hand on the small of Lena's back, light.

ETHAN
She'll go because I'm asking, not because my mother is. Let me make it up to you tomorrow night. Whatever you want.

LENA
I want fifteen minutes of quiet and a glass of water that isn't sparkling. That's it.

ETHAN
Done.

He kisses the side of her head, fast. Lena's smile softens for the first time.

## S#3. Dr. Brandt's Clinic — Consultation Room · Evening

[VISUAL/ACTION]
A boutique aesthetic clinic — pale marble, recessed lighting, a long mirror running the length of the consultation wall. Lena sits on the edge of a treatment chair. DR. CELIA BRANDT — early-40s, white coat over a slate blouse, caramel bob — taps a tablet, doesn't quite meet her eyes.

[KEY CAMERA]
- MCU Lena in the chair, the mirror visible just over her shoulder
- ECU Celia's hand hesitating on the tablet
- OTS toward the corridor through the open door

[DIALOGUE]
CELIA
Mrs. Cross has been very specific. A small filler under the jaw, ten units along the brow. We're done in forty minutes.

LENA
I'm not Mrs. Cross yet. And I'd like to think about it.

CELIA
Of course. Take a moment. I'll be back.

Celia steps out. Lena exhales, leans her head against the back of the chair, eyes drifting to the mirror.

[VISUAL/ACTION]
In the mirror's reflection — through the open doorway into the corridor — a woman walks past. Same height as Lena. Same ink-black hair, fixed half-up the exact way Lena wore hers to last week's gala photo. Wearing the cream silk dress Lena had hanging in her closet for tomorrow's reception. The woman pauses in the doorway. Looks straight into the mirror. Same face. Lena's face. She smiles — a small, calculating curl of the mouth that Lena has never made.

[KEY CAMERA]
- ECU Lena's reflection, eyes widening
- OVER-the-shoulder push to the doorway reflection: the other woman, identical
- ECU on the woman's mouth — the smile
- Whip-pan as Lena turns to look at the actual doorway — empty

[DIALOGUE]
LENA
(quiet, to herself)
What.

She's on her feet. She crosses to the doorway. Looks both ways down the empty corridor. Nothing. A door clicks shut somewhere off-screen.

## S#4. Coastal Road — Inside Lena's Car · Night · Rain

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Hard rain. Lena's black sedan winds along a cliffside road above the harbor. She has one hand on the wheel, the other touching the necklace at her throat. Headlights flare in her rearview — close, then closer. A heavier vehicle, riding her bumper.

[KEY CAMERA]
- ECU Lena's eyes flicking to the rearview
- POV through windshield — wipers slapping, taillights of nothing ahead
- ECU her hand tightening on the wheel
- OTS through the rear window — the second car's headlights filling the frame

[DIALOGUE]
LENA
(into hands-free)
Ethan. Pick up. There's somebody right on me out here.

ETHAN (V.O. — VOICEMAIL)
You've reached Ethan Cross —

LENA
(quiet, swallowing it)
Okay. Okay.

[VISUAL/ACTION]
The chasing car lurches forward. Metal hits metal. Lena's sedan fishtails on the wet road. She fights the wheel. The car spins through the guardrail like it was paper. Headlights swing across black sky, then across black water. The car drops out of frame.

[KEY CAMERA]
- INSERT — the necklace clasp giving way at her throat, the pendant lifting
- ECU Lena's face, blood at her temple, eyes closing
- Wide — the car hitting the rocks below, one headlight still on

## S#5. Cliffside Road — Above the Wreck · Night · Rain

[VISUAL/ACTION]
The guardrail twisted open. Rain on asphalt. A woman in heels steps out of the chase car. Same body. Same hair. We don't see her face yet. She walks unhurried to the edge of the broken rail, crouches. Her left wrist comes into the frame — a heavy diamond bracelet catching the headlight.

[INSERT/CUTAWAY]
On the wet asphalt — Lena's mother's necklace. The chain snapped, the gold-diamond pendant lying face-up in the rain.

[KEY CAMERA]
- ECU the bracelet sliding down her wrist as she reaches
- ECU her gloved fingers closing around the pendant
- ECU her thumb wiping rain off the gold
- Tilt up — finally her face. Lena's face. Calm. Dry-eyed.

[DIALOGUE]
MARA
(quiet, to herself)
You always wore it like it was just a necklace.

She straightens. Drops the pendant into her coat pocket. Looks down the cliff, once, without expression. Walks back to her car.

[VO]
MARA (V.O.)
I'm not stealing — I never was. What I'm taking back has been mine since the day she was born.

[END HOOK]
Hold on Mara's hand sliding into her pocket, the bracelet catching one last gleam of the headlight. Behind her, far below, Lena's single working headlight flickers and dies.

[GRAPHIC/UI]
LOWER THIRD — black, white text:
**THE BRIDE WAS NEVER ME.**
**SHE STOLE MY FACE. EP 02.**

Hard Cut.

# SHE STOLE MY FACE — EP02

## S#1. Harbor General Hospital — Room 412 · Morning

[VISUAL/ACTION]
A semi-private hospital room. Cheap blinds half-drawn against grey morning light. Lena lies in the bed — white cotton gown under an ivory cashmere coat someone draped over her, gauze taped above her right temple, an IV taped to the back of her left hand. The TV mounted in the corner is on, muted, tuned to a morning business segment. She doesn't move yet. Her eyes open.

[KEY CAMERA]
- ECU her eyes opening, ceiling tiles overhead
- ECU her bare throat — no necklace
- CU her free hand touching where the pendant should be
- MCU Lena pushing herself up against the pillows

[VO]
LENA (V.O.)
The first thing I touch is my throat. Before I even feel the bandage. Before I even know what city I'm in. That's how I know something is wrong before anything else.

## S#2. Hospital Room — Continuous

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Lena swings her legs over the edge of the bed. Pain hits her ribs — she hisses, presses one hand to her side. A NURSE — mid-30s, scrubs — pushes through the door with a chart.

[KEY CAMERA]
- Two-shot the nurse at the bed
- ECU Lena's hand on the call button cord she hasn't pressed

[DIALOGUE]
NURSE
Hey hey. Don't move yet. You came in three nights ago — cliff road, fishermen flagged the wreck at dawn. You have a concussion and three cracked ribs and you got very lucky. Don't blow it now by standing up.

LENA
Three nights. Where's my phone? Where's my necklace?

NURSE
You were brought in with nothing. No bag, no ID. The state troopers ran the plates — Sterling, Lena, twenty-nine, registered to an address in Cross Park. We've been trying that number all morning. No one's picked up.

LENA
Try Ethan Cross. C-R-O-S-S. That's my fiancé.

NURSE
Honey — the announcement was last night. We had it on in the break room. Mr. Cross is engaged.

LENA
Yes. To me.

The nurse's face does something polite and careful. She glances at the TV.

NURSE
Let me get the doctor.

She leaves quickly.

## S#3. Hospital Room — Continuous

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Alone, Lena fumbles the remote off the bed tray. Unmutes the TV. The channel cycles into a daytime celebrity recap. A glossy host stands in front of a step-and-repeat.

[GRAPHIC/UI]
LOWER THIRD on the TV broadcast:
**CROSS INDUSTRIES — ENGAGEMENT ANNOUNCED LAST NIGHT**

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Cut to the broadcast footage. Ethan Cross in a charcoal suit on a low stage. Beside him — a woman. Same face as Lena. Hair half-up the same way. Wearing the cream silk dress Lena had hanging for the reception. Mother's gold-diamond necklace at her throat.

[KEY CAMERA]
- INSERT — the TV screen, the engagement broadcast, Mara's face beside Ethan
- ECU push-in on the necklace at Mara's throat
- ECU Lena's eyes, wide, still
- CU Lena's hand crushing the bedrail

[DIALOGUE]
TV HOST (V.O. — FROM SCREEN)
— and after months of speculation, the city's most eligible heir confirmed it last night. Cross Industries CEO Ethan Cross will marry Lena Sterling this Saturday. The reception will be held at the Cross family estate, and sources say —

LENA
(quiet)
That's not me.

She sits forward. Pain through her ribs. She ignores it.

LENA (CONT'D)
That is not me.

## S#4. Hospital Room — Phone Call · A Minute Later

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Lena has the bedside phone receiver wedged against her shoulder. She's dialing Ethan's mobile from memory. Two rings. Three. It picks up.

[KEY CAMERA]
- ECU Lena's mouth at the receiver
- ECU her free hand braced flat on the blanket

[INTERCUT]
HOSPITAL ROOM: Lena waiting, holding her breath.
CROSS MANOR BREAKFAST ROOM: Mara — same face as Lena, hair half-up, mother's necklace at her throat, robe open over a slip — picking up Ethan's phone from the breakfast table. Ethan reads the paper across from her. She glances at the screen. Her face does nothing.

[DIALOGUE]
LENA
Ethan. Ethan, listen to me. I'm at Harbor General, I'm in a room, I don't know how to make them believe me — please come, just come and get me, okay?

MARA
(smooth, into the phone, eyes on Ethan)
I think you've got the wrong number.

LENA
What? What — Ethan?

MARA
This is Lena Sterling's phone. There's no one named Ethan here. Don't call again.

She hangs up. Smiles at Ethan. Slides the phone face-down beside her cup.

ETHAN
Who was that?

MARA
Wrong number. Some woman crying. I almost felt bad.

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Back on Lena. The dial tone in her ear. She stares at the receiver. Sets it down carefully, as if it might break if she lets it fall. Her hands are shaking. They stop.

[VO]
LENA (V.O.)
Whoever picked up that phone — she didn't say I love you. She didn't say where are you. She said wrong number, and she sounded like me.

## S#5. Hospital Room — Five Minutes Later

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Lena rips the IV tape off the back of her hand with her teeth. Pulls the cannula out, fast. A bright line of blood. She presses the cotton of her gown against it. Throws the cashmere coat over her shoulders. Slides off the bed. Bare feet on linoleum. She walks to the door, opens it a crack, looks out at the corridor — busy, gurneys, nurses. Closes the door. Reopens it. Steps out.

[KEY CAMERA]
- ECU her hand on the IV tape
- INSERT — the cannula coming free, the drop of blood
- ECU her bare feet on the cold floor
- MCU low angle, Lena in the doorway, coat over hospital gown
- Wide — the corridor stretching away, nobody noticing her yet

[END HOOK]
Lena standing in the open doorway, barefoot, blood at her temple, the corridor noise behind her. Her chin lifts. She steps out.

[GRAPHIC/UI]
LOWER THIRD:
**SHE'S WEARING MY FACE TO MY ENGAGEMENT PARTY.**
**SHE STOLE MY FACE. EP 03.**

Hard Cut.

# SHE STOLE MY FACE — EP03

## S#1. Cab — Headed to Cross Manor · Evening · Rain

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Pouring rain. A yellow cab on a wide avenue, Lena in the back seat — hospital gown under the oversized ivory cashmere coat, hair tangled to one side, gauze still taped above her right temple, bare feet. She holds a phone to her ear — not hers. The driver's. She pressed cash into his hand for it and he glances at her in the rearview every ten seconds.

[KEY CAMERA]
- MCU Lena in the back seat, phone to her ear
- ECU her bare feet on the rubber floor mat
- CU the driver's eyes in the rearview
- INSERT — her hand opening the phone screen to a photo of herself at last year's gala, holding it like an exhibit

[DIALOGUE]
LENA
(into phone)
Tessa. Tessa, it's Lena. I know your phone's saying I'm calling from a city number — that's because I am. I'm in a cab. I'm okay. I need you to meet me at Cross Manor in fifteen minutes. Don't tell anyone you spoke to me. Please.

She lowers the phone. Listens. Lowers it further. Looks at the screen — call ended.

LENA (CONT'D)
(quiet)
She hung up on me.

[VO]
LENA (V.O.)
Tessa Hale has been my best friend for nine years. She's never hung up on me. Not once. Not when I told her my mother was dying. Not even when I told her I was marrying into the Cross family.

DRIVER
Lady, the gate's coming up. You sure about this? You don't look like a party.

LENA
Just stop at the gate. I'll do the rest.

## S#2. Cross Manor — Main Gate · Evening · Rain

[VISUAL/ACTION]
The cab stops fifteen feet short of the gate. Lena climbs out into the rain. Cashmere coat darkening at the shoulders. Bare feet on wet stone. Two SECURITY GUARDS — earpieces, dark suits — step out of the gatehouse. Through the gate, the manor glitters with white lights, valets running cars. A red carpet stretches up to the door.

[KEY CAMERA]
- Low wide — Lena walking toward the gate, headlights sweeping past her
- Two-shot the guards stepping forward to block her
- ECU her hand bracing on the gate post
- OTS over a guard's shoulder to her face

[DIALOGUE]
GUARD #1
Ma'am. This is a private function. Step back.

LENA
I'm on the guest list. My name's Lena Sterling.

GUARD #2
(checking his tablet, glancing up, glancing back down)
Ma'am, Ms. Sterling is inside. She arrived ninety minutes ago with the family.

LENA
Look at me. I have ID — I lost it but I have a photo. There's a photo of me at the gala last June, look —

She thumbs the cab driver's phone open. The photo: Lena in a black evening dress at the Sterling Foundation Gala. Same face she's wearing now under the gauze.

GUARD #1
(barely looking)
Ma'am, the woman inside has the same face in that picture. Step back from the gate.

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Lena's hand stays on the gate post. She doesn't move. The guards exchange a look. One of them murmurs something into his earpiece.

## S#3. Cross Manor — Foyer · Evening

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Inside, the foyer glitters. Guests in evening wear. A small chamber group playing. Ethan, in his charcoal three-piece, stands near the staircase with Mara on his arm — cream silk dress, mother's gold-diamond necklace at her throat, the diamond bracelet on her left wrist. Victoria approaches them with a clipped expression. Murmurs something. Ethan's brow furrows.

[KEY CAMERA]
- MCU Ethan, Mara, Victoria at the foot of the stairs
- ECU Victoria's mouth at Ethan's ear
- ECU the diamond bracelet sliding on Mara's wrist as she sets a hand on Ethan's forearm

[DIALOGUE]
VICTORIA
(low, to Ethan)
There's a woman at the gate. She says she's Lena. The guards say she has the face. She's barefoot and bleeding.

ETHAN
Some woman has a knockoff face and they let her walk up the driveway?

MARA
(soft, to Ethan, hand still on his arm)
Ethan. There's a tabloid that's been writing about us all month. I think someone's done surgery to look like me. It's been done before — that actress in Seoul last year, remember? Please don't speak to her. Let security handle it. For me.

ETHAN
(to Victoria)
Get her off the property. Don't let it touch the press.

He kisses the top of Mara's head. Mara's eyes track over his shoulder to the front windows, calm.

## S#4. Cross Manor — Front Steps · Continuous

[VISUAL/ACTION]
The gates have opened just enough to let Lena through onto the manor's marble front steps. She crosses the wet stone, coat open, gauze loose at her temple. Ethan steps out of the foyer onto the top step. The two guards close behind Lena, flanking her. She stops two feet from him.

[KEY CAMERA]
- Low angle — Ethan at the top of the steps, light at his back
- Reverse — Lena at the bottom, drenched
- ECU Ethan's eyes scanning her face
- ECU Lena's hand reaching, not touching him

[DIALOGUE]
LENA
Ethan. It's me. I crashed my car coming home from Brandt's clinic on Tuesday night — somebody ran me off the cliff road. I just woke up this morning. I'm in a hospital gown under this coat. Look at me.

ETHAN
(quiet, careful)
I don't know who you are. But if you came here for money, or for a story, or for whatever — turn around. Walk back to the gate. I'm not going to call the police because my fiancée's inside and I don't want her night ruined. But you walk away right now.

LENA
Your fiancée is standing in my dress, wearing my mother's necklace. Ethan — I gave you the keys to my apartment in March. The one in the kitchen drawer. Go check.

ETHAN
(beat — a flicker, then nothing)
Goodbye. Don't come back.

He turns. He goes back inside. The door closes behind him. The lights from the foyer stay on the wet steps a moment, then dim as the curtains are drawn from inside.

[KEY CAMERA]
- Slow push on Lena alone on the steps
- Wide — the manor lit up behind her, indifferent
- ECU her face, jaw tight, no tears

[VISUAL/ACTION]
The two guards step in. One on each side. One takes her elbow.

GUARD #1
Let's go, ma'am.

LENA
Don't touch me. I'll walk.

[VISUAL/ACTION]
She doesn't get the choice. They walk her down. Not roughly — efficiently. The way you remove a stain. They deposit her at the gate, on the public side. The gate closes. The chamber music inside starts up again, audible through the wet air.

## S#5. Outside the Gate · A Minute Later · Rain

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Lena stands at the curb in the rain. The cab is gone. Cars sweep past on the road, headlights raking her. She doesn't move. She doesn't sit. She doesn't cry. She just stands.

[KEY CAMERA]
- Wide — Lena small at the curb, the lit manor behind the gate behind her
- ECU her bare feet on cold wet asphalt
- MCU her face, rain running into the gauze

[VO]
LENA (V.O.)
He didn't look at the photo and he didn't ask about the keys in his drawer. He had already decided which one of us was real. Fine. I'll start there.

[VISUAL/ACTION]
A black SUV pulls up beside her. The window slides down. NOAH KEENE — mid-30s, granite jaw, dark sweater under a midnight-blue investigator coat — leans out an inch. He doesn't smile. He doesn't introduce himself.

[KEY CAMERA]
- Side-on MCU Noah at the driver's window
- ECU his eyes — direct, not pitying
- Reverse on Lena, rainwater on her face, her hand still at her side

[DIALOGUE]
NOAH
Ms. Sterling. I've been watching the gate for an hour. I think I'm the only person in this city right now who thinks you're you.

LENA
Who are you?

NOAH
Someone the Crosses hired to watch you three months ago. I quit twenty minutes ago. Get in. Or don't. But the second car coming up that driveway is press, and you don't want to be on the front of tomorrow's paper as a story Victoria gets to write.

[END HOOK]
Hold on Lena's hand reaching for the door handle. Her bracelet wrist — bare, no bracelet, no necklace. Her fingers close on the handle. The door clicks open. Behind her, through the gate, the engagement reception's chamber music carries on.

[GRAPHIC/UI]
LOWER THIRD:
**ONE MAN BELIEVES HER.**
**SHE STOLE MY FACE. EP 04.**

# SHE STOLE MY FACE — EP04

## S#1. Noah's Office — Keene Investigations · Late Night

[VISUAL/ACTION]
A converted warehouse loft above the harbor, glass walls separating a clean working bay from a small lounge. Two monitors on Noah's desk. A coffee machine running. Lena — now in clean borrowed clothes, dark trousers, a grey crewneck, hair down and damp from a shower — sits in the lounge chair wrapped in a blanket. A fresh white bandage on her temple. Noah hands her a mug and sits across from her on the edge of the coffee table, leaving the chair for her.

[KEY CAMERA]
- Two-shot Noah seated across from Lena, the city lights through the glass behind him
- ECU Lena's hand wrapped around the mug
- MCU Noah, elbows on his knees, easy posture

[DIALOGUE]
NOAH
The doctor I called is a former Navy nurse. She comes here, she doesn't ask questions, she's gone in twenty minutes. Your ribs are wrapped, your temple's clean, you've got two weeks of pain meds in the bathroom. Nobody at Harbor General knows you walked. They think a confused patient discharged herself. Which — technically — you are.

LENA
Why are you doing this.

NOAH
Because Victoria Cross hired me to follow you for ninety days starting last August. I took the job, I sat outside your apartment, I sat outside your office, I sat outside your mother's grave on the anniversary. I have ninety days of footage of who you are. And the woman on the front of every paper this morning — that's not her.

LENA
You knew before they dragged me off the steps.

NOAH
I knew before I rolled the window down.

[VO]
LENA (V.O.)
For eight years since my mother died I've been the only person in the world who knows me. I forgot what it sounds like when someone else says it out loud.

## S#2. Noah's Office — At the Desk · Continuous

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Noah moves to the desk. Lena follows. Noah pulls up a file on the left monitor — Coastal Patrol incident report, plate photos, scene shots from Lena's wreck. Cliffside, broken guardrail, the car on the rocks below.

[KEY CAMERA]
- OTS Lena's POV over Noah's shoulder, the monitor
- ECU Lena's eyes on the photos
- ECU Noah's cursor scrubbing through video stills

[DIALOGUE]
NOAH
Tuesday night, eleven forty-six, your sedan went off the cliff road at the Eastpoint guardrail. The state has it down as solo loss-of-control in heavy rain. I pulled the dashcam off a delivery truck that was twenty seconds behind you. Watch.

He plays a clip. Lena leans in. Rain. Her sedan in the distance. A heavier SUV closing the gap. Then ramming her. The sedan spinning. The delivery truck slamming its brakes. Frame freezes.

LENA
That's not a loss of control.

NOAH
No. And there's a second clip I want you to see. From the same dashcam, eight minutes later. The truck driver stopped on the shoulder to call it in. The chase vehicle came back.

He plays it. Headlights on a wet road. A figure stepping out of the SUV. Heels. A coat. A gloved hand at the edge of the broken guardrail. The figure crouches.

[KEY CAMERA]
- INSERT — the frame freezes on the gloved wrist, the diamond bracelet catching headlight
- ECU Noah pulling a frame-grab box around the wrist
- ECU Lena's face, light from the screen on her cheekbones

NOAH (CONT'D)
You see the bracelet?

LENA
I see it.

NOAH
You ever own one like that?

LENA
I've never bought one like that. I've seen one like that.

## S#3. Noah's Office — Two Monitors · A Minute Later

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Noah swivels to the right monitor. Pulls up a social media account — @MARA.VOSS, set to public. Old posts — three years back. Brunch photos. Concert tickets. A selfie at a rooftop bar in Manhattan, a heavy diamond bracelet on the left wrist, the same wrist.

[KEY CAMERA]
- Split screen on the second monitor — left: the dashcam still, wrist with bracelet. Right: the Instagram photo, wrist with bracelet.
- ECU on the bracelet in both frames — identical
- MCU Lena's profile, lit by the screens

[DIALOGUE]
LENA
That's Mara. Mara Voss. We met at Brown. We were roommates junior year. I bought her that bracelet for her twenty-fifth birthday. I picked it out at a vintage place in Providence. It's the only one of it that exists.

NOAH
Mara Voss. M-A-R-A.

LENA
She moved back to the city six years ago. We had dinner once a month. We had drinks last Friday. She was in my wedding party. She was supposed to be standing on my side of the aisle on Saturday.

NOAH
She's standing on the other side of the aisle now. Wearing your face.

[VO]
LENA (V.O.)
I had been waiting for it to hurt. It hadn't yet. It feels like the room going quiet right before bad news lands.

## S#4. Noah's Office — Lena Speaks · A Minute Later

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Lena straightens. Steps away from the monitors. Stands at the glass, looking out at the city. Noah lets her have the moment. She turns back.

[KEY CAMERA]
- Wide — Lena at the glass, the city lights spread out below
- MCU her turning back to Noah
- Two-shot the two of them at the desk again

[DIALOGUE]
LENA
She was at every dress fitting. She knew which clinic Victoria was sending me to that night. She knew the road I'd take coming home. She knew the necklace I'd be wearing because I tell her things. I tell her everything.

NOAH
She had help. People don't pull off this kind of impersonation alone. Surgery on that timeline takes a doctor who's willing to break protocol. It takes someone inside your daily life feeding her your schedule. It takes someone who can fake or kill a clinic intake record.

LENA
Dr. Brandt at the cosmetic clinic. And — I don't want to say her name yet.

NOAH
You don't have to. But I'm going to find it either way. Tell me when you're ready.

## S#5. Noah's Office — Final Beat · Late Night

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Noah turns the laptop fully toward her. Left half of the screen: the cliffside dashcam wrist with the bracelet. Right half: Mara's Instagram wrist with the bracelet. Lena lifts her hand. Sets her palm flat against the screen, over both images.

[KEY CAMERA]
- ECU Lena's hand against the glass screen, covering both wrists
- Slow push on her face — bandage at the temple, eyes clear
- MCU two-shot from a low angle, Noah watching her

[DIALOGUE]
NOAH
What do you want to do.

LENA
I want my name back and my face back. And I want her in the same room I was supposed to be in on Saturday, in front of every person who let her wear me, watching her come apart.

NOAH
That's the answer I was hoping for.

LENA
Why are you asking?

NOAH
Because if you'd asked me to call the police I'd have had to tell you they'd ask Victoria first who Lena is, and Victoria would have told them the wrong answer, and you'd have spent a year in court being a fraud. We're going to do this the other way.

[END HOOK]
Hold on Lena's hand against the screen, covering both wrists. The Mara-on-Instagram bracelet on the right. The Mara-from-the-cliff bracelet on the left. Her palm between them. Behind her, on the desk, Noah closes his laptop. The screens go dark. Her hand stays a beat longer, alone in the reflection.

[GRAPHIC/UI]
LOWER THIRD:
**SHE GAVE HER THE BRACELET. SHE'LL TAKE IT BACK.**
**SHE STOLE MY FACE. EP 05.**

Hard Cut.

# SHE STOLE MY FACE — EP05

## S#1. Dr. Brandt's Clinic — Service Entrance · After Hours · Night

[VISUAL/ACTION]
The clinic at eleven at night. Service entrance off a back alley. A janitorial van parked. Lena and Noah at the rear door — both in grey maintenance coveralls, ball caps low, Lena's hair in a tight low ponytail under the cap. Lena holds a janitor's keycard, lifted from a third party. Noah carries a small toolbag.

- CU Noah glancing at his watch

[DIALOGUE]
NOAH
Cameras run on a thirty-minute loop overnight. You and I have twenty-three minutes before the cleaning company that owns this card actually shows up on the schedule. We get in, we get the registry, we get out. Quiet feet.

LENA
What if Celia's there?

NOAH
She's at the symphony with her husband. I bought their dinner two tables over an hour ago to make sure. She'll be home by midnight. Move.

[VO]
LENA (V.O.)
Three weeks ago I would've been mortified at the thought of impersonating a janitor to break into a doctor's office. Right now it feels like the most honest thing I've done all year.

## S#2. Clinic — Front Office · Continuous

[VISUAL/ACTION]

- CU Noah at the doorway, his hand resting on the doorframe

(typing)
Search by patient name. Sterling, comma, Lena.

The screen returns a clean record — last appointment, two weeks before the crash, marked CANCELLED.

LENA (CONT'D)
There it is. I had a consult two weeks before. They have me down as a cancellation.

NOAH
You didn't cancel.

LENA
No. I sat in that chair on Tuesday night. Dr. Brandt called me in personally. That's how I saw Mara through the door.

Lena clicks into the cancellation note. The system shows a signoff field. Initials. A name in small font under the initials.

Tessa Hale.

The friend you wouldn't say last night.

She works the front desk at Brandt's clinic three afternoons a week. She had her at-home dog grooming side gig going wrong, she needed cash, I — I'm the one who got her this job. Six months ago. I asked Celia as a favor.

NOAH
Pull the file. Yank it on the USB.

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Lena drags. The file copies. Noah glances at his watch.

## S#3. Tessa's Apartment — Front Door · Twenty Minutes Later

[VISUAL/ACTION]
A modest one-bedroom in a five-story walkup. Lena rings the bell. Noah stands one step behind her, off-frame to the peephole. After a long pause, the door cracks open against a chain. TESSA HALE — late-20s, auburn waves, oversized cardigan, eyes red-rimmed — peers out. She freezes.

[KEY CAMERA]
- ECU Tessa's eye in the gap
- Reverse on Lena's face, calm, hat off, bandage at her temple
- MCU Tessa's hand on the chain — shaking

[DIALOGUE]
TESSA
(barely audible)
Oh God. Oh God, oh God.

LENA
Open the door, Tess.

TESSA
You were on the news. As her. She was on the news. I didn't — I thought you were —

LENA
Open the door.

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Tessa fumbles the chain. The door swings open. Lena steps in. Noah follows.

## S#4. Tessa's Apartment — Living Room · Continuous

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Living room. Wine bottle open on the coffee table, two glasses, one used. Tessa wraps her cardigan tighter around herself, doesn't sit. Lena scans the room. Walks straight to the sofa. Lifts the cushion at the corner where the upholstery's worn. Underneath — a stack of envelopes, return address ATLANTIC CITY, the corner of one torn open showing a printed amount in the five figures. Lena lifts the stack out. Holds it.

[KEY CAMERA]
- Tracking Lena to the sofa
- ECU her hand under the cushion
- INSERT — the stack of debt notices, top envelope, ATLANTIC HOLDINGS COLLECTIONS
- MCU Tessa's face crumpling

[DIALOGUE]
LENA
How much did you owe.

TESSA
(quiet)
Two-eighty.

LENA
Two hundred eighty thousand dollars.

TESSA
I — I was going to tell you. I was going to tell you after the wedding. I was going to ask. I tried at the lake house in February, you remember, I tried —

LENA
You tried to ask me for money. And then you tried something else.

TESSA
She paid them off. The whole stack. In one wire. Last September. She just — she came up to me at the bar at the Whitlock, she said she knew about the debt. She said she could make it go away. And all I had to do was send her a calendar. That's how it started. A calendar. Just the calendar.

LENA
And then?

TESSA
The clinic schedule. Once. And then — and then the necklace. I told her about the inscription. I'm so sorry. I'm so sorry, Lena, I'm so —

LENA
Don't.

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Tessa sits down hard on the rug. Pulls her knees up. Lena doesn't move to her. She holds the envelope stack a beat longer. Sets it on the coffee table. From her pocket she pulls a folded printout — the bank wire transfer slip, dated September, Mara's account number, Atlantic Holdings as the receiver.

[INSERT/CUTAWAY]
INSERT — the wire slip on the coffee table. SENDER: M. VOSS. The amount: $283,400.

## S#5. Tessa's Apartment — Door · A Minute Later

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Lena puts the wire slip in her coat pocket. Crosses to the door. Noah's hand is already on the knob. Tessa scrambles up off the rug.

- MCU Lena at the door, hand on the frame
- Tessa's reflection in the dark window — small, ruined

[DIALOGUE]
TESSA
Lena — please. Please. I'll testify. I'll do anything. I'll tell Ethan. I'll tell the press. I'll go on camera tomorrow morning.

LENA
You'll do exactly what I tell you to do and not a second before I tell you to do it. If you warn her, if you tell anyone — I'll bury you, Tess. And I won't be sorry.

TESSA
Okay. Okay. Okay.

LENA

[VISUAL/ACTION]

- ECU Lena's hand on the railing, smooth, no shake
- MCU Noah glancing sideways at her face

[VO]
LENA (V.O.)
Nine years. Junior year roommate. Maid of honor as of three weeks ago. The first person to hold me when my mother died. She gave me away for a calendar.

[END HOOK]
On the street outside. Lena stops on the curb. Pulls the wire slip out of her pocket. Holds it up to the streetlight, MARA VOSS visible across the top. Behind her, Tessa's apartment light goes out, then on, then out. Lena slides the slip back into her pocket. Doesn't look back.

[GRAPHIC/UI]
LOWER THIRD:
**SHE WASN'T A STRANGER. SHE WAS SUPPOSED TO BE FAMILY.**

There.

There's an inscription. My mother had it carved when her diagnosis came in. She didn't tell anyone. Not my father, not my aunts, not me — not until the night before she went into the hospital. She told me she wanted me to know what she was leaving me for.

NOAH
What does it say.

LENA
Lumen meum. It's Latin. It means my light.

NOAH
She told it to you and no one else.

LENA
No one. Not even my father knew. He sold the house six months after she died and the necklace went with me. Mara never had it in her hands until the cliff.

LENA

Done. There's a notary three blocks from here who owes me a favor and doesn't talk.

LENA
I want it filed somewhere she can't touch. With your lawyer. With my mother's old lawyer too — Sam Reilly, he kept her trust. I want two copies in two cities. If we move on her Saturday and she runs, I want her caught in the airport with the necklace in her bag, and I want a piece of paper with my hand and yours on it, dated before, that says what's inside it.

NOAH
Understood.

- ECU his hand still under hers

I got a uniform, a lanyard, and bridal-room clearance — two of each, actually. One for me. One for whoever wants it.

You're giving me a way to walk into my own engagement reception as a member of the catering staff.

If a tech gets sloppy with the patch bay during testing, that bridal room camera goes live to all six screens. Saturday, between six-forty-five and seven, somebody will get sloppy. I'll see to it.

That's the part I'm still building. Be ready Saturday afternoon. I'll have your folder for you by then.

Two o'clock. Lena's been on her feet for six hours, moving linens, plating canapés, looking unremarkable. She hangs up. Stands at the window a moment. Then turns toward the staircase to go back up. As she pivots, the morning light from the window catches her left ear — and below it, two centimeters under the jawline, the faintest curved scar, well-healed but visible at this angle.

[VISUAL/ACTION]
The portfolio opens to a printed cover page. The legal name printed in plain office font fills the top third of the page: **MARA EILEEN VOSS**. Underneath, a date of birth, a passport number, a clinic file number, and a row of small thumbnail photographs. The thumbnails are not Mara as she looks now. They are an earlier face — a different jawline, broader at the angle, a wider mouth, the same moss-green eyes.

Lena's hands have stopped shaking. She had not noticed they were shaking. She turns the page.

LENA (V.O.)
Mara Eileen Voss. The surgeon who'd cut her face into mine was the same woman who had tried to put filler under my jaw the night I crashed.

She turns the page again. A printed ledger from the Halcyon Trust — a name she has only ever seen on her mother's old paperwork. The ledger lists three wire transfers across eighteen months. The last transfer is dated three weeks before the crash on the cliffside road.

LENA
(quiet, to no one)
Three weeks before. They booked her face before I was even off the road.

[VO]
LENA (V.O.)
The wire had cleared three weeks before my car went through the railing. They'd booked her face in advance.

She turns the page once more. Behind the ledger sits a single page on heavier paper — a notarized statement on the trust's letterhead, signed at the bottom in three trustees' hands. Two of the names she knows. Victoria Cross. Celia Brandt. The third name is in a smaller, older hand. She does not read it yet. She closes the cover. She does not put the portfolio down.

## S#3. Cross Manor — Ballroom Main Stage · Six-Forty-Six PM

[VISUAL/ACTION]
The ballroom is full — three hundred guests in formal wear, a string quartet finishing, the giant screen above the stage running the engagement-night montage on a slow dissolve. ETHAN CROSS, charcoal three-piece, slim platinum band on his hand. VICTORIA CROSS in deep wine silk to one side. MARA, in the cream silk engagement gown, gold leaf comb in her hair, the stolen necklace at her throat, the diamond bracelet on her left wrist, mounts the small step to the podium. The applause is polite, expectant.

[KEY CAMERA]
- Wide of the ballroom, the giant screen alive behind the stage
- MCU Mara approaching the podium, gown catching the lights
- ECU the surgical scar two centimeters under her left ear, hidden by the angle of the comb
- OTS Mara to the crowd, hundreds of upturned faces

[DIALOGUE]
MARA
(at the mic, warm)
Good evening. Thank you for being here tonight. I had a small speech written and I have decided not to read it. I want to say one thing only. I, Lena Sterling, choose this family. I will keep choosing it every day, for as long as you'll let me.

Polite applause warms into something fuller. Ethan's mouth moves into a slow, relieved smile. Victoria's hand finds her own pearl choker, settles.

[KEY CAMERA]
- MCU Ethan smiling — the first uncomplicated smile he's worn all week
- CU Victoria's hand at her throat, then dropping
- Wide — the ballroom applauding

## S#4. Cross Manor — Tech Cage Two · Six-Forty-Eight PM

[VISUAL/ACTION]
A young AV tech leans into the patch bay. He's holding a coffee in one hand and a printed run-of-show in the other. He frowns at the yellow tag on the cable marked BRIDAL CAM C. He glances at the matrix slot two rows up, MAIN ARRAY A. The yellow-tag cable is plugged into it. He doesn't remember doing that. He shrugs — must have been Diego.

[KEY CAMERA]
- ECU the yellow tag, plugged into MAIN ARRAY A
- ECU the tech's frown — and the shrug
- INSERT — his radio on the console, going off
- The tech turns to take the call, takes his coffee, steps out of the cage, leaves the patch as it is

[INTERCUT]
PATCH BAY: empty. The yellow cable hot in the wrong slot.
BRIDAL ROOM CAMERA: a single small red light on the camera in the corner has just turned on.

## S#5. Cross Manor — Bridal Room C · Six-Forty-Eight PM

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Lena looks up. The little camera in the upper corner of the bridal room — the red record-light is glowing. She holds the portfolio open against her chest with both hands, the cover page facing the lens. She does not speak yet. She steps closer to the camera. Closer. She holds the page up until MARA EILEEN VOSS fills the lens.

[KEY CAMERA]
- ECU the red record-light, hot and steady
- Wide reverse — Lena alone in the suite, holding the open file up
- ECU the lens — MARA EILEEN VOSS filling its glass
- POV from the camera: Lena's face above the file, calm

[VO]
LENA (V.O.)
I had imagined this differently — a microphone in my hand, a room of people in front of me. Instead I have a printed file and a camera bolted to a corner. It will do.

She does not look away from the lens.

## S#6. Cross Manor — Ballroom Main Stage · Six-Forty-Eight PM

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Mara, still at the podium, raises her champagne flute. The applause is rolling down. She is mid-toast. Behind her, the giant screen is still scrolling the engagement-night montage. The image flickers once — a dissolve catching wrong. The montage cuts to black for half a beat. Then the giant screen comes alive again, but with a different feed.

The bridal-room camera signal fills the wall behind her. **POST-OP FOLLOW-UP — VOSS, M.** The legal name in office font, six feet tall. The thumbnails of an earlier face. The line that says C. BRANDT, MD. The wire transfer date.

[KEY CAMERA]
- MCU Mara mid-toast, the screen lighting up behind her in a color she does not see
- Wide of the ballroom — the front rows seeing it first
- ECU a guest in the front row, her champagne flute lowering an inch
- ECU another guest's mouth opening, no sound
- Pan along the rear of the room, phones rising
- ECU Victoria's face. Recognition.
- ECU Ethan's face. He turns his head, slow.

[VISUAL/ACTION]
The room goes quiet in a way ballrooms are not supposed to go quiet. A polite ripple of confused laughter from one corner dies the moment it starts. Six seconds in, the gasps come in slow waves from the back to the front. Mara still has not looked up at the screen. She is still mid-toast smile. Ethan, at the foot of the stage, has fully turned now. He is looking at the screen, not at her.

[DIALOGUE]
MARA
(into the mic, still warm, not yet hearing it)
— and I want to thank Victoria especially, who has been —

She catches the silence. Something is wrong in the room. She follows the eyes of the front row. Slowly, almost lazily, she turns her head. Looks up. Looks at the giant screen.

The full color drains out of her face in a way that does not photograph well.

## S#7. Cross Manor — Bridal Room C · Continuous · Six-Forty-Eight PM

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Lena lowers the file. She keeps her body facing the camera. She steps to the small vanity, leans, and turns on the bridal-room microphone — a wireless lapel mic on the mirror, on a charging station. She clips it to her collar. The light on the charger blinks from green to white. The mic is live. The room speaker system was patched to the main stage's backup audio twelve minutes ago, when the AV crew tested levels.

[KEY CAMERA]
- ECU the lapel mic clipping to Lena's grey waistcoat
- ECU the charging light going white
- MCU Lena facing the lens, file held loose at her side, her free hand smoothing the front of the waistcoat
- ECU the red record-light, steady

## S#8. Cross Manor — Ballroom Main Stage · Continuous

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Mara, frozen at the podium, the screen behind her holding on the legal name. Ethan takes one step toward the stage and stops. Victoria takes one step backward and stops. The string quartet has gone silent. Three hundred guests are holding their breath.

Then — over the ballroom speakers, clear and unhurried — a voice they have all heard before and not heard for fourteen months. Lena's voice. Lena's actual voice. Not Mara's slightly-too-fast imitation. Lena's.

[KEY CAMERA]
- ECU the giant screen — MARA EILEEN VOSS holding, the thumbnails of the earlier face visible at the corner
- ECU Mara's face — the smile dying frame by frame
- ECU Ethan's hands at his sides, one knuckle whitening on his cuff
- ECU Victoria's eyes — closing for one long second
- Wide of the ballroom — every face turned to the screen
- INSERT — at the lower corner of the giant screen, the live red record-light, glowing

[DIALOGUE]
LENA (V.O. — OVER BALLROOM SPEAKERS)
Hi, Mara. I think there are a few things this room would like to discuss with you before you finish that toast.

A beat. The room does not move.

LENA (V.O. — OVER BALLROOM SPEAKERS)
You can put the glass down. We're going to be here a minute.

[END HOOK]
Hold on the giant screen. MARA EILEEN VOSS in six-foot office font, the thumbnails of the earlier jaw at the corner, the live red record-light burning in the bottom edge. In the foreground, the back of Mara's head, the gold leaf comb, the stolen necklace at her nape. Her shoulders drop one quarter-inch as Lena's voice fills the room a second time, two floors above, in a suite the cameras can find now whether Mara wants them to or not.

[GRAPHIC/UI]
LOWER THIRD — black, white text:
**WATCH WHAT SHE STOLE. EP 09 NOW.**

Hard Cut.

# SHE STOLE MY FACE — EP09

## S#1. Engagement reception. Main hall. Night.

[VISUAL/ACTION]
The stage. White peonies along the edge, gold-lit. Mara in the cream silk engagement gown, microphone in her hand, smile half-locked. Ethan a half step behind her, glass of champagne lowered. Victoria Cross at the side of the stage, the picture of composed approval. Two hundred guests on the floor. Above the stage, the LED screen that was meant to play the engagement montage. The montage has stopped. Frozen on a single document.

The document on the screen: a clinic intake form. Patient name written in clean black ink — MARA EILEEN VOSS. Surgery date. Attending physician's signature, DR. CELIA BRANDT. The pre-op photograph in the upper right corner — a young woman with a different face. Mara's old face.

Mara turns. Sees it. Her mouth opens. No sound comes.

A side door at the back of the hall. Lena walks in. Grey staff uniform, hair pulled back, BRIDAL ROOM keycard still clipped at her hip. She carries a paper folder in both hands. She moves toward the stage without hurrying.

[KEY CAMERA]
- Wide push: stage, frozen screen, Mara at center
- ECU: the printed name MARA EILEEN VOSS
- OTS Mara: Ethan's lowering glass
- Low tracking: Lena's shoes on the parquet, advancing
- CU: Lena's hand on the folder, steady

[DIALOGUE]
GUEST (off-mic, audible)
Is that — is that a different name?

VICTORIA
(quietly, to the floor manager)
Cut the feed. Now.

FLOOR MANAGER
Ma'am, the channel's locked. We can't pull it down.

Mara recovers half a beat. She lifts the microphone, smile clicking back.

MARA
That's a sick joke. Someone's idea of a sick joke. Please ignore — please ignore the screen.

She turns to find the source. She sees Lena on the floor. Her hand goes to the necklace at her throat.

[END HOOK]
LENA (calm, carrying)
Don't touch it. You don't get to touch it anymore.

## S#2. Engagement reception. Main hall. Continuous.

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Lena keeps walking. Reaches the steps. Goes up onto the stage like she belongs there — because she does. Security at the side of the stage lifts a hand to intercept her. Noah, standing along the wall in a dark suit, gives a small shake of his head. The security man stops where he is.

Lena reaches Mara. She does not look at her yet. She turns to the room.

[KEY CAMERA]
- MCU: Lena center-stage, folder in both hands
- OTS Lena: the sea of faces below, phones already raised
- CU: Noah at the wall, jaw set, hand low at his side, signal small
- ECU: Mara's clasp hand at the necklace, fingers frozen on the gold

[DIALOGUE]
LENA
(into the silence, no mic, voice carrying)
My name is Lena Sterling. I'm the woman this engagement was supposed to be for. The woman beside me is Mara Voss. She had her face cut to look like mine fourteen months ago, at the Brandt Aesthetic Clinic, under a fake referral signed by my friend.

She opens the folder. Reads from the top sheet without looking up.

LENA
Patient name on file — Mara Eileen Voss. Procedure — full facial reconstruction to template patient three-one-eight. Template patient three-one-eight is me. Attending physician — Dr. Celia Brandt. Signed and dated April of last year. Three months before my engagement was announced.

MARA
She's lying. She's some — she's some kind of obsessed —

LENA
(still not looking at her)
There's a second page. Surgical photo log. Day one through day forty-five. If anyone in this room wants to see what her face looked like before, the screen is already showing it. Look up.

Heads turn. The LED has cycled to the post-op log: bandages, sutures, the gradual approximation of Lena's face onto a different woman's bones. Phones are out across the entire floor.

[GRAPHIC/UI]
Screen, cycling: BRANDT AESTHETIC — PATIENT #M.E.V. — POST-OP DAY 12 / DAY 24 / DAY 45.

## S#3. Engagement reception. Stage. Continuous.

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Mara breaks. She lunges for the folder. Lena steps back once, clean, and the folder goes behind her. Mara's hand catches air. After Lena has named the file, the stage lights swing — and the thin curved scar two centimeters under Mara's left ear catches the beam. Confirmation only.

A woman in the second row says, audibly, *oh my god.* A man near the bar lowers his phone, then raises it again to film.

Victoria moves to come up onto the stage. Ethan stops her with one hand at her elbow. He has not said a word since the screen changed. He's looking at the necklace.

[KEY CAMERA]
- CU: Mara's empty hand, the lunge that missed
- ECU: the surgical scar at her jaw under the stage light
- OTS Ethan: the necklace at Mara's throat — diamond and gold
- MCU: Victoria stopped by Ethan's hand on her elbow

[DIALOGUE]
MARA
(low, only to Lena)
You think you can do this in front of all of them. You don't know what you're walking into.

LENA
(low, only to Mara)
I've already walked in. You're the one still on the wrong side of the door.

ETHAN
(finding his voice)
The necklace.

MARA
(turning, smile cracking)
Ethan —

ETHAN
The necklace at your throat. I want you to take it off.

MARA
This is — this is mine. You gave me — your mother said —

ETHAN
Take it off, Mara.

Mara's hand goes to the clasp. Her fingers shake. They will not work the catch. The hall is silent enough to hear the LED hum.

[INSERT/CUTAWAY]
ECU: Mara's fingers at the clasp. Her nail catches on the gold. The clasp does not open.

[END HOOK]
ETHAN
(to Lena, looking at her for the first time in nine months)
Your necklace. Take it back.

LENA (V.O.)
That was the first time he had looked at me since the night of the crash. I had thought I would feel something. Nothing landed.

Hard Cut.

# SHE STOLE MY FACE — EP10

## S#1. Private antechamber off the main hall. Night.

[VISUAL/ACTION]
A small reception room behind the stage — gold-and-cream wallpaper, a long bench, three armchairs. Mara is on the bench. Cream silk gown wrinkled now, hair half-undone, one shoe off. The diamond bracelet on her left wrist catches the chandelier light. She is crying. Real tears. The sound that comes out of her is closer to a child than a woman.

Lena stands across from her, folder still in her hand. Noah by the door, hand on the handle. Ethan a few feet behind Lena, jaw locked. Victoria in the armchair, very still, watching her son and not her future daughter-in-law.

Three of the closest family witnesses — a senior partner, two cousins — are in the room. They have stopped looking at their phones. They are watching Mara cry.

[KEY CAMERA]
- Wide: room, four poles of attention — Mara, Lena, Ethan, Victoria
- CU: Mara's tears, mascara intact (cried on cue before)
- OTS Mara: the witnesses, faces softening half a degree
- CU: Noah's hand on the door handle, knuckles white

[DIALOGUE]
MARA
(to the witnesses, not to Lena)
She did this to me. (a hitched breath, a glance at Victoria) She did this to me. We were friends since we were sixteen. She got engaged to him and I was — I was supposed to be the maid of honor and instead she ran her car off a road and now she wants my life back and she's pinning it on me. Look at her — she's wearing a uniform, she snuck in here, she's not well —

COUSIN (LEWIS)
(quietly, to Victoria)
Aunt, maybe — there's two sides to this. We should hear her.

VICTORIA
(not looking at him)
Lewis. Sit down.

LENA
(no heat, no rush)
Mara. There's one more sheet in this folder. I haven't shown anyone yet. I think you should hear it before I do.

MARA
(through tears, sharper now)
You're enjoying this. You always wanted me to need you.

LENA
I didn't want any of this. But I'm not putting the folder down.

## S#2. Same room. Continuous.

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Lena opens the folder. Slides one page out. Hands it to Victoria, not to Ethan. Victoria takes it like it might cut her. She reads. Her face does not move for the first two seconds. Then something at the corner of her mouth tightens — just once.

The page: a name-change petition filed in family court. Petitioner — MARA EILEEN VOSS. Requested new legal name — LENA MARIE STERLING. Filing date — fourteen months before the engagement reception. Petition status — voluntarily withdrawn after the accident, by petitioner.

[KEY CAMERA]
- ECU: petition header, NAME CHANGE PETITION, the requested-name field
- CU: Victoria's eyes, scanning, stopping at the filing date
- OTS Victoria: Mara across the room — Mara realizes which page it is
- CU: Mara's hand going slowly back to her own face, as if checking that it's still there

[DIALOGUE]
VICTORIA
(reading, not aloud, then quietly to Mara)
You filed to take her name. Three months before the engagement.

MARA
That was — that was a long time ago. That was before we knew —

VICTORIA
You filed to take her name *before* my son proposed to her. Before any of us had met you.

MARA
You don't understand —

VICTORIA
(flat, final)
I understand the date on this page, Mara.

The cousin Lewis looks away. The senior partner stands up and walks to the window. The room has changed temperature.

## S#3. Same room. Continuous.

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Ethan steps forward. He reaches up to Mara's throat. His hands are not gentle, but he doesn't yank. He works the clasp at the back of the necklace himself. It opens this time. The gold-and-diamond chain comes free in his hand. He turns. Holds it out to Lena.

Lena does not move.

[KEY CAMERA]
- MCU: Ethan's hands at the back of Mara's neck, working the clasp
- ECU: the clasp opening
- CU: the necklace in Ethan's palm, dangling
- MCU: Lena's eyes on the necklace, then up to Ethan's face, then past him

[DIALOGUE]
ETHAN
Lena, it's yours — take it.

LENA
I'll take it. Not from your hand.

A beat. Ethan does not understand. Then he does, and his hand sinks a little.

NOAH
(quiet, from the door)
Mr. Cross. Let me.

He crosses to Ethan. Ethan looks at him — registers him for the first time as something other than security — and lets him take the necklace. Noah turns. Holds it open, both clasps in his fingers, the way a jeweler offers a piece. Not draping it for her. Offering it.

Lena steps up to him. Lifts her hair. He fastens the clasp at the back of her neck. He does not touch her skin more than he has to. He steps back as soon as it's done.

Lena's hand goes to her throat. Feels the weight. Closes around the pendant. The inscription, hidden in the metal — *LUMEN MEUM* — pressed against her palm.

[INSERT/CUTAWAY]
ECU: Lena's hand closed around the pendant, knuckles white, gold visible between her fingers.

## S#4. Same room. A minute later.

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Mara has gone quiet. She is sitting straight now. Tears stopped. Whatever the next move is, she is putting on the mask for it. Victoria stands. Walks past her without looking at her. Stops in front of Lena.

[KEY CAMERA]
- MCU: Victoria approaching Lena, two paces, then stopping
- CU: Lena's hand still at her throat
- OTS Lena: Victoria's face — controlled, but the bones of an apology are forming

[DIALOGUE]
VICTORIA
(low, only to Lena)
We'll have that conversation, Lena. Privately. Not in this room.

LENA
We'll have it.

Victoria nods once. Leaves. Ethan stays. Looks at Lena. Tries to take half a step closer.

ETHAN
Let me explain. Please. Five minutes.

[END HOOK]
LENA
You had three years, Ethan. You don't get five minutes tonight.

She turns. Noah opens the door for her. They walk out together. Mara is left on the bench with a senior partner who is no longer pretending to look at anything but his phone.

Hard Cut.

# SHE STOLE MY FACE — EP11

## S#1. Service corridor behind the reception hall. Night.

[VISUAL/ACTION]
The wings of a venue you weren't supposed to see. Concrete walls, painted cream. The fire exit at the end. Two uniformed officers stand outside the antechamber door, talking with an event manager. Lena and Noah walk past them. Lena's hair is still up, the necklace back at her throat, but she has pulled a long charcoal coat over the staff uniform. Noah keeps half a step ahead.

The lead officer turns when they pass.

[KEY CAMERA]
- Tracking low: their two pairs of shoes on concrete
- OTS officer: the back of Lena's head, necklace catching corridor light
- MCU: Noah half-turning to address the officer

[DIALOGUE]
OFFICER (DET. RYAN)
Ms. Sterling. I'm going to need a statement from you tonight, if that's possible.

NOAH
Tomorrow morning. She'll come in with counsel. You have my word.

OFFICER (DET. RYAN)
Mr. Keene — your name keeps appearing in my reports today.

NOAH
That's because I keep handing your reports things. Tomorrow morning.

The officer accepts it. Lena gives him a small nod — not a smile, just acknowledgment. They keep walking.

LENA
(low, to Noah)
He thinks I orchestrated this.

NOAH
He thinks something doesn't add up about you. By tomorrow he'll be glad you exist.

## S#2. Noah's apartment. Night.

[VISUAL/ACTION]
A converted loft on the river side of the city. Industrial windows, the city below, a long worktable with three monitors, a couch, a kitchen island with two coffee mugs already on it. Not a man-cave, not styled — lived in. Books in piles, not on shelves. A jacket on the back of a chair.

Lena steps inside. Looks once at everything. Lets the long coat slide off her shoulders onto the back of the chair. Noah closes and locks the door. He moves to the kitchen, pulls a clean glass, fills it with water, hands it to her without asking.

[KEY CAMERA]
- MCU: Lena's first look at his home, neutral, taking it in
- CU: Noah filling the glass — his hands, steady
- OTS Lena: Noah crossing to her, glass extended

[DIALOGUE]
LENA
You live above your work.

NOAH
I live in my work. There's a difference.

LENA
Not a healthy one. Even by investigator standards.

NOAH
No. Not yet, anyway.

She takes the glass. Drinks half of it. Sets it down on the kitchen island. Looks at the worktable.

LENA
What am I looking at?

NOAH
The thing I should have shown you a week ago, but I wanted to be sure. Come here.

## S#3. Noah's apartment. Worktable. Continuous.

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Noah pulls a chair out for her at the worktable. She sits. He brings up two windows side by side on the middle monitor. On the left — a wire transfer, six figures, dated nineteen months ago. On the right — a corporate registration document. Both stamped with the same bank emblem at the top.

[KEY CAMERA]
- ECU: the bank emblem on both documents
- MCU: Noah's hand on the mouse, Lena's hands flat on the table
- CU: the wire-transfer recipient field — HALCYON TRUST
- OTS Lena: Noah at her shoulder, not crowding her, near enough to point

[DIALOGUE]
NOAH
I told you I started this job working for Cross Industries. Victoria hired me to keep an eye on you. Then on Mara. Then to put together a profile that would make the engagement risk-clean.

LENA
You told me. The first night.

NOAH
What I didn't tell you. About four weeks in I pulled a wire transfer that wasn't part of the brief. Six figures, paid to a clinic-operations holding company that turned out to be Brandt Aesthetic's billing arm. Paid by a thing called Halcyon Trust.

LENA
That's a name I don't know.

NOAH
That's a name I'd never seen either. So I started pulling it. Halcyon Trust is registered in the Caymans, but the funding source is a private bank in New York. The Whitford. Top floor, by appointment only.

LENA
(quiet, recognizing)
My mother used the Whitford.

NOAH
Your mother used the Whitford for the Sterling family trust. Some of the assets that were in that trust before she died are now in Halcyon. Not all. Some.

LENA
So Mara's surgery was paid out of an account that has my mother's money in it.

NOAH
Yes.

Lena does not react with surprise. She sits with it. Her hand goes to the necklace at her throat — not for comfort, just to feel where it is.

LENA
My mother died when I was twelve. Whoever's running Halcyon now isn't her.

NOAH
No. Whoever's running Halcyon now is someone who knew about the trust before your mother died, and stayed close to it after.

## S#4. Noah's apartment. Worktable. Continuous.

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Noah pulls up a third document — a beneficiary list. Five names. Four of them visible. The fifth is a solid black bar across the page where a name should be. Lena leans in toward the screen. Her hand goes to the edge of the table.

[KEY CAMERA]
- ECU: the beneficiary list, the four visible names
- MACRO: the redacted fifth line — the black bar, the kerning still visible underneath
- CU: Lena's hand at the edge of the table, gripping
- OTS Lena: Noah's face, watching her, not the screen

[DIALOGUE]
LENA
The four names I can see — those are people I knew growing up. My mother's accountant. Her lawyer. Two of her oldest friends.

NOAH
That's who I thought, but I'd rather you confirm it.

LENA
Who's behind the black bar?

NOAH
I haven't been able to find out. The redaction was filed by the trust itself, with the bank's compliance officer signing off. I've been at it for six days. I can't crack who that fifth name is.

LENA
You're telling me a name is missing because someone went to court to keep it missing.

NOAH
Someone went to court, or someone owns the court. Either is possible at the Whitford.

A beat. Lena looks away from the screen. Out the long window. The city below, gold and dark.

[VO]
LENA (V.O.)
My mother had four people in her life who were close enough to be in a trust like that. I was the fifth.

[END HOOK]
LENA
(turning back)
Whoever's behind that black bar — my mother knew them. And she didn't tell me.

NOAH
That's what I think.

LENA
Then I need to know what she did tell me. Everything she ever wrote down. Tomorrow we open her storage. Tonight I'm going to try to sleep on this couch and not in the question.

Hard Cut.

# SHE STOLE MY FACE — EP12

## S#1. Brandt Aesthetic Clinic. Reception. Morning.

[VISUAL/ACTION]
A high-end clinic gone quiet. The front desk has a hand-printed sign — TEMPORARILY CLOSED — taped to the glass. Inside, the receptionist is packing a box of personal items. Two unmarked black SUVs are parked across the street. A reporter is on the corner with a microphone, watching the door.

Lena and Noah cross the street. Lena in the slate wool coat over a charcoal turtleneck, hair in a low knot, necklace under the collar. Noah in the dark coat from last night, camera bag at his hip. He scans the SUVs as they pass.

[KEY CAMERA]
- Wide: clinic exterior, the SUVs, the reporter on the corner
- MCU: receptionist taping a box shut
- OTS Lena: the closed-clinic sign on the glass, her own reflection over it

[DIALOGUE]
NOAH
(low)
Two from federal. One in the brown coat is from the medical board.

LENA
Then we're third in line for her today.

NOAH
We're first in line. The brown coat got her at six AM, federal an hour ago. She asked for me to come at ten. We're here at ten.

LENA
She asked for *you*.

NOAH
She asked for whoever I was working for. I told her last night who that was.

## S#2. Clinic. Dr. Celia's private office. Morning.

[VISUAL/ACTION]
A small consult room. Cream walls. A desk with nothing on it except a phone and a leather appointment book. Dr. Celia Brandt sits behind the desk in a slate blouse — no white coat today. The silver pen is gone from her pocket. Her hair is freshly cut. The mask of a put-together professional is up but it's a thinner mask than it was a week ago.

Lena sits across from her. Noah stays standing, by the door. Lena sets a single sheet of paper on the desk between them.

[KEY CAMERA]
- MCU: Celia behind the desk, hands flat on the leather
- OTS Celia: Lena sitting straight, hands in her lap
- CU: the single sheet sliding across the leather — Lena's hand placing it

[DIALOGUE]
LENA
Doctor. Three people are coming for you today. I'm the only one who isn't going to make you wish you'd left town last week. Tell me what you did. And tell me who paid you.

CELIA
(careful, measured)
Ms. Sterling. Whatever happens to me, I want you to know I didn't —

LENA
Don't tell me what you didn't. Tell me what you did.

A beat. Celia looks at the paper Lena slid across — not the page in front of her, but somewhere inside herself. She breathes out. Some of the mask comes off.

CELIA
Eleven women. Over five years. Different cases. Different reasons. Each one came in with a reference photograph and a budget and a request that we now both know I should have refused.

LENA
Where are they now? The other ten.

CELIA
Two of them are dead. Three left the country. The other five — I don't know. I stopped looking. I was paid well to stop looking.

LENA
By whom?

CELIA
Different people, each time. I think. I never met any of them in person. The wires came through the same account each time. Different beneficial owners on paper, same routing number underneath.

NOAH
The Whitford. Halcyon Trust.

Celia's head turns slightly toward him. The first real reaction.

CELIA
You already have the routing number.

NOAH
I have it. I don't have a face for it.

## S#3. Clinic. Celia's office. Continuous.

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Celia opens the leather appointment book on the desk. Not the current one — a battered one from underneath the new one, water-stained at the bottom corner. She turns to a page near the back. Slides a small color photograph out from between the pages. Three by five inches. Pushes it across the desk to Lena.

[KEY CAMERA]
- CU: Celia's hand opening the old appointment book
- ECU: the photograph emerging — face down at first, then turned
- OTS Lena: the photograph in her hand, slightly trembling
- MACRO: the image — a woman in a charcoal coat walking out of a stone-fronted building, head half-turned to the right, profile catching the light

[DIALOGUE]
CELIA
I asked her once. After the third surgery. I asked her to come in person, just once. She came. She didn't take off her sunglasses. She paid in cash. As she was leaving the bank, I — I followed her one block. With my phone. I took this from across the street and I never told anyone I had it.

LENA
(looking at the photograph)
You kept it.

CELIA
I kept it in case I ever needed to prove I wasn't her. I am not the woman in that picture. I'm the woman who did what she paid me to do.

Lena does not speak for a long moment. Her hand on the photograph does not shake exactly — it tightens. Noah moves one step closer to the desk. Looks at the photograph over her shoulder. Looks at Lena's face. Says nothing.

[VO]
LENA (V.O.)
The first thing I felt was that I knew the coat. Not the woman. The coat. My mother had a coat like that. I bought one like it when I was twenty-four because I missed her.

LENA
(to Celia, evenly)
When the time comes, will you say all of that again with me in the room?

CELIA
Yes. If you stand there while I do.

LENA
I'll be there.

## S#4. Outside the clinic. Morning.

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Lena and Noah on the sidewalk. The reporter has spotted them and is moving across the street. Noah angles his body slightly, blocking the line of sight to Lena. Lena holds the photograph against her side, screen forward, hidden in the seam of her coat.

[KEY CAMERA]
- MCU: Lena, photograph hidden against her side
- OTS Lena: Noah half-shielding her, his back to the reporter
- CU: Lena's hand on the photograph, knuckles pale

[DIALOGUE]
NOAH
(low, walking)
You recognized her. I saw it.

LENA
I'm not going to say it yet.

NOAH
Not even to me?

LENA
Especially not to you. If I say it out loud here and I'm wrong, I burn the only person who could ever tell me the truth.

NOAH
And if you're right?

LENA
Then I'm going to want to sit down somewhere before I say it.

He nods. Doesn't push. Steers her past the reporter with a hand at her back — light, professional, hand off as soon as they clear the corner.

[INSERT/CUTAWAY]
ECU: the photograph in Lena's hand, edge of the woman's profile visible, sunglasses, a hint of pearl at her ear.

[END HOOK]
LENA (V.O.)
I sat in the car for a long time before I told him. I needed to be sure my mouth could say her name without breaking on it.

Hard Cut.

# SHE STOLE MY FACE — EP13

## S#1. Police interview suite. Observation room. Day.

[VISUAL/ACTION]
A long mirror onto a small interview room. On the other side of the glass — Tessa Hale, twenty-eight, hair pulled back in a messy ponytail, cardigan over a coffee-stained cream camisole. No mole-covering makeup today. Her hands are wrapped around a paper cup she isn't drinking from.

The observation side — Detective Ryan, Lena, Noah. Lena stands close to the glass but does not touch it. She is wearing the slate coat. Necklace at her throat. Hair still in the low knot from yesterday.

[KEY CAMERA]
- Wide: observation room, three figures, glass wall
- MCU: Tessa through the glass, alone with the paper cup
- OTS Lena: the back of her head, glass between her and her oldest friend
- CU: Lena's hand at her side, not touching the glass

[DIALOGUE]
DET. RYAN
She came in voluntarily this morning. She's not under arrest. She's asking for you.

LENA
She doesn't want me. She wants to apologize to me so she can sleep.

DET. RYAN
She'll talk to me first. Then to you. Then her lawyer's coming and we lose her. You'll get fifteen minutes.

NOAH
You don't have to do this today.

LENA
Yes I do. The longer I wait, the more she rehearses.

## S#2. Police interview suite. Interview room. Day.

[VISUAL/ACTION]
The small room. Two chairs, a table, no window. Lena sits across from Tessa. Noah stays outside the door at Lena's request. Detective Ryan stands at the corner of the room, low-key.

Tessa looks at Lena's face and starts crying within the first three seconds. Real crying — not Mara's calibrated thing from the antechamber. Her shoulders go. Her hands come up to her mouth.

[KEY CAMERA]
- MCU two-shot: Lena across from Tessa, table between them
- OTS Lena: Tessa breaking on contact
- CU: Tessa's hands, ring-less, shaking around the paper cup

[DIALOGUE]
LENA
Tessa. I'm not here to forgive you today. I'm here to find out what you handed her. Walk me through it.

TESSA
Lena, I'm so —

LENA
Walk me through it.

Tessa lowers her hands. Wipes her face with the cuff of the cardigan. Tries to find a voice that works.

TESSA
She came to me first — almost two years ago, before any of you knew she existed. She had pictures of you, your schedule for the next quarter. She knew about my debts before I did. The bookie's name. The number.

LENA
How much.

TESSA
By the time she came it was almost three hundred thousand. They were going to start sending video to my father's office. Mara had the video already. She showed me the first frame on her phone in a coffee shop on Hudson Street.

LENA
So you traded.

TESSA
I traded. I gave her your scans. Your medical history. The dates you'd be away from the apartment in May and June. The clinic referral. I signed the referral, Lena. I signed your name to a referral I knew was going to a face surgeon.

## S#3. Same room. Continuous.

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Lena does not move. Her face does not move. Her hand, resting on the table, opens once and closes once. That is the only motion. Tessa watches the moment like she knows what's coming.

[KEY CAMERA]
- ECU: Lena's hand on the table, open, close
- CU: Tessa watching the hand
- OTS Tessa: Lena's eyes, not on her, on a point on the wall just past her

[DIALOGUE]
LENA
Twelve years.

TESSA
I know that already.

LENA
We slept on each other's floors in college. You held my hair the night of my mother's anniversary —

TESSA
Lena, please don't —

LENA
I'm not doing this to hurt you. I'm doing it because I need to hear myself say it. Twelve years and you decided I was the cheaper way out.

TESSA
I didn't decide. I was —

LENA
You decided. Don't pretend you didn't. You decided I was easier to lose than your father's good opinion. I want you to know that I heard you decide that. Okay?

Tessa nods. The crying has stopped, replaced by something colder and more accurate.

TESSA
She knew. About the debt. About me. About my father. She knew it all before she ever knocked. I keep going back to that. Who tells you a thing like that. Who *finds* you like that.

LENA
That's the right question.

## S#4. Same room. Continuous.

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Tessa leans forward. The motion is small but Det. Ryan registers it and takes one step closer. Tessa does not look at him. She looks at Lena.

[KEY CAMERA]
- MCU: Tessa leaning in, voice dropping
- CU: Lena's eyes coming back from the wall to Tessa's face
- OTS Lena: Tessa's face, lowered

[DIALOGUE]
TESSA
She mentioned a name once. Only once. I asked her how she had so much money and she laughed and said *Halcyon takes care of me, Tess.* She said it like — like Halcyon was a person, not a thing. Then she said something else. She said *my mother told me about it. A long time ago. Before she died.*

LENA
Her mother.

TESSA
That's what she said. Her mother told her about Halcyon. Before her mother died.

A long beat. Lena does not turn to look at Det. Ryan or at the glass. She holds Tessa's eyes.

LENA
(low, careful)
Did she say her mother. Or did she say my mother.

TESSA
I — Lena, I'm trying to remember exactly —

LENA
Take your time. I need you to say it right.

TESSA
(slowly)
She said *my* mother. I'm sure she said *my.* But the way she said it — she paused before the word. Like she had to choose which one.

[INSERT/CUTAWAY]
ECU: Lena's hand, the one she opened and closed earlier, going to her throat. Resting against the necklace.

[VO]
LENA (V.O.)
There are two women that word could mean. Tessa didn't know that yet. I had known it for one day.

[END HOOK]
LENA
(standing, even)
Thank you, Tess. Your lawyer's on his way. Don't talk to anyone else until he gets here. Including me.

She walks to the door. Stops with her hand on it. Does not turn around.

LENA
And if you ever get out of this — don't look for me. I'm not coming.

She goes out. Tessa stays at the table, hands flat now, looking at nothing.

Hard Cut.
