# SHE STOLE MY FACE — EP14

## S#1. Private dining room. Restaurant Ardent. Evening.

[VISUAL/ACTION]
A private room off the main floor of an old French restaurant. Two banquettes, one round table, candles in lowball glasses. The maître d' has cleared the floor — there is no second party tonight. Ethan sits already, charcoal three-piece, tie loosened by exactly one finger. A ring box on the table, closed. A folded sheet of paper next to it.

The door opens. Lena comes in alone. She is wearing the midnight-blue silk dress she would have worn to the rehearsal dinner a year ago — the dress was in storage. She let the housekeeper bring it to her this afternoon and she put it on because she wanted Ethan to see her in it once and then never again.

She takes the seat across from him. Not next to him. Lays her clutch on the table. Folds her hands.

[KEY CAMERA]
- Wide: empty restaurant, two figures alone at a small table
- MCU: Ethan, ring box and paper in his eye line
- OTS Ethan: Lena entering in the blue silk, settling
- CU: Lena's hands folding on the white cloth

[DIALOGUE]
ETHAN
You came.

LENA
I almost didn't.

ETHAN
What changed your mind.

LENA
You owe me an hour. I'd rather take it tonight than have you stand outside my building for the next three weeks trying to get it.

ETHAN
That's fair.

LENA
It's the only fair thing on the table.

A waiter sets two glasses of water and leaves. Ethan does not touch the ring box. He does not touch the paper. He looks at her across the candle for a long second before he speaks.

## S#2. Same room. Continuous.

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Ethan slides the folded paper across the table to her. Lena does not open it.

[KEY CAMERA]
- CU: Ethan's hand pushing the page across
- OTS Lena: the page in front of her, untouched
- ECU: Ethan's hand returning to his own glass, fingers tight on the stem

[DIALOGUE]
ETHAN
That's a public retraction. Drafted by our communications team. It runs in the Times tomorrow morning if you say yes. It states, on the record, that the engagement was procured by fraud. That the woman who appeared at the reception last week was not you. That my mother and I and the family endorse you as my fiancée and were misled. It restores you to the Sterling chair on the joint board. There's a second page my mother signed. There's a third page from our counsel that says we will pay every dollar of your legal costs through any proceeding that comes out of this, including any against my own family.

LENA
And the ring box?

ETHAN
The ring you returned to my mother three years ago. We never gave it to her. I have wanted to give it back to you since the morning I realized my mother had let the wrong woman come down a staircase.

LENA
When did you realize.

ETHAN
The morning your friend's signature came back on the clinic referral. Three weeks ago. I have known for three weeks, Lena, and I sat on it because I wanted to be the one who handed it to you. That was selfish. I'm telling you it was selfish so I don't have to lie to you tonight about anything else.

She does not open the paper. She does not look at the ring box. She picks up her water glass with two fingers at the stem. Sets it down again without drinking.

## S#3. Same room. Continuous.

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Lena lifts her eyes. The candle catches one side of her face. She does not raise her voice — she doesn't need to. The room is built for this kind of conversation.

[KEY CAMERA]
- MCU: Lena across the table, candle between them
- OTS Lena: Ethan, the retraction and the box still untouched between his hands
- CU: Ethan's hand at the base of his water glass, fingers shifting once

[DIALOGUE]
LENA
The night of the accident — do you remember what we'd been arguing about that afternoon? You probably don't. I was driving back from the dressmaker. The radio was playing that song from the dinner party in Provence — the one with the woman singing about a window. The headlights were coming the wrong way down the lane. The last thought I had before the car turned over was — I wasn't sure anymore. Not about the accident. About marrying you.

ETHAN
(quiet)
Lena —

LENA
I want you to hear me. The accident took the chance to figure it out away from me. Mara took what was left. By the time I came out of the bandages, I didn't get to be the one who chose anymore. Somebody else had already chosen for me. *Twice.*

ETHAN
You're saying you would have left.

LENA
I'm saying I was deciding. That is all I ever got to be. The deciding was what Mara took, more than anything else.

ETHAN
I would have heard you. If you'd come to me that week — if you'd said you weren't sure. I would have —

LENA
You would have what.

ETHAN
I would have waited.

LENA
And if I'd said no?

A beat. Ethan doesn't answer. He looks down at the white cloth. He picks up his water and drinks from it because there is nothing else to do with his hand.

LENA
That's what I thought.

## S#4. Same room. Continuous.

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Ethan reaches across the table. Not far. Just to the middle. His palm turns up. He leaves his hand there, open, waiting. Lena looks at his hand. Then at his face. She does not move her own hands from where she has them. She lets them stay flat on the table cloth, on either side of the unopened retraction.

[KEY CAMERA]
- ECU: Ethan's open palm at the center of the table
- OTS Ethan: Lena's two flat hands, on either side of the page
- MCU: the space between his hand and hers, candle in the gap
- CU: Lena's face, unmoved

[DIALOGUE]
LENA
I'll take the retraction. Run it. I'll take the Sterling chair back because it's mine. I'll let your family pay my legal bills because *you owe me that.* I won't take the ring.

ETHAN
You don't have to take it tonight.

LENA
I'm not going to take it on a different night either, Ethan. I'm telling you now so you don't spend another year of your life waiting in a hallway.

ETHAN
There's someone else.

LENA
There's someone who saw me before any of you let yourselves see me. That's not the same as there being someone else — but yes, there is.

ETHAN
The investigator finally caught up to me. That's what hurts the most.

LENA
His name is Noah. Use it the next time you have to.

ETHAN
(low)
I'm going to need a minute, Lena.

LENA
Take it. I have the time tonight.

She does. She sits with him in the silence for nearly a full minute. She doesn't look at her phone. She doesn't look away. She lets him have the bad quiet. When his eyes come back up, she stands.

[INSERT/CUTAWAY]
ECU: Ethan's open palm, still at the center of the table, fingers curling slowly into his own. The retraction beside his hand. The closed ring box.

[END HOOK]
LENA
(at the door, not turning back)
The dress wasn't for you. I wanted to leave the apartment wearing something I picked out before any of this. Goodnight, Ethan.

She goes. He stays in the room with the candle and the closed box and his own hand on the table. He does not move for a long time.

Hard Cut.

# SHE STOLE MY FACE — EP15

## S#1. Cross Industries press atrium. Morning.

[VISUAL/ACTION]
A high-ceilinged atrium on the ground floor of Cross Industries headquarters. A long podium has been wheeled in. The Cross corporate emblem along the back wall. Three rows of reporters in folding chairs. Cameras along the back. The atrium is full but disciplined — no shouting, no jostling. This is choreographed.

Victoria Cross at the podium in charcoal Chanel-cut tweed, baroque pearl choker at her throat, hair in the same severe French twist. To her right, half a step back — Lena Sterling in the midnight blue silk evening dress from last night, mother's necklace at her throat. To Lena's right, half a step further back — Ethan, also in dark suit, neutral, not looking at his mother and not looking at Lena.

[KEY CAMERA]
- Wide: atrium, podium, three figures, press array
- MCU: Victoria at the podium, hands flat on either side of the mic
- OTS Victoria: the press rows, dozens of phones up
- CU: Lena half a step behind Victoria, watching her in profile

[DIALOGUE]
VICTORIA
(measured, into the microphone)
Good morning. I am Victoria Cross. I am here to speak on behalf of our family and our company. Eleven days ago at an engagement reception in this city, a woman named Mara Voss appeared on a stage and stood beside my son under a name that was not hers. The name she was wearing belongs to the woman standing on my right. Ms. Lena Sterling.

She pauses. Looks at the press, not at the cameras, at the actual people in the chairs.

VICTORIA
I want to say this plainly, on the record, without language designed by attorneys. The Cross family, and I personally, were deceived. We were deceived in ways we should have caught and did not. We were not deceived because Lena Sterling was hard to find. We were deceived because we did not look hard enough at the woman in front of us. I have known Lena since she was twenty-three. I missed the truth about her replacement for fourteen months, and that is on me.

A small motion in the press rows — pens moving faster.

VICTORIA
Effective today, Cross Industries reinstates Ms. Sterling as the Sterling family seat-holder on our joint board. We will cover, in full, every cost — legal, medical, personal — associated with the year she lost. And we will cooperate, in full, with the criminal investigation into the parties who arranged the deception. There will be no protection extended to anyone whose name appears in that inquiry. Including any that bear ours.

She steps back. Half-turns.

VICTORIA
Ms. Sterling.

## S#2. Same. Continuous.

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Lena steps up to the podium. She does not adjust the microphone. She does not look at the cameras. She speaks like she's speaking to one person at the back of the room.

[KEY CAMERA]
- MCU: Lena at the podium, necklace catching the lights
- OTS Lena: the press rows, quieter now
- CU: Lena's hand on the side of the podium, steady

[DIALOGUE]
LENA
Thank you. I have one statement and then I will not be taking questions today. I am not interested in being the story of a woman who came back. I am the same person I was before the accident. The only difference is that more of you know my name now. That is a difference my mother would have hated, so I will keep it short.

A small ripple. A reporter in the second row almost smiles.

LENA
The investigation that's ongoing is not about me. It is about a series of decisions made by a small number of people over the last twenty months. I trust the people running that investigation. I will not be commenting on it again until it concludes. Thank you for coming.

She does not wait for follow-up. She steps back. The Cross press lead steps up to close the conference. The cameras swing.

[VO]
LENA (V.O.)
I had practiced longer remarks on the drive in. When I got to the podium I cut them in half. I wanted Mara watching this on a television somewhere. I didn't want her to feel like she had earned a full speech.

## S#3. Victoria's office. Afternoon.

[VISUAL/ACTION]
The matriarch's office, twelve floors up. Window the length of the wall. Heavy oak desk, two upholstered chairs across from it. Victoria seated, very still, baroque pearls still at her throat. Lena sits across from her, coat folded on her lap. The door is closed. Just the two of them.

[KEY CAMERA]
- Wide: the office, two women across the desk
- MCU: Victoria, the controlled posture
- OTS Victoria: Lena across from her, coat in her lap
- CU: Victoria's hand on a folded envelope on the desk

[DIALOGUE]
LENA
You did the press conference well. Better than I expected. I want to ask you a question that won't be in the conference.

VICTORIA
Ask.

LENA
Why did you protect her so long.

A beat. Victoria's hand does not move from the envelope.

VICTORIA
I'll give you the honest answer, even though it isn't flattering to me. May I have it anyway?

LENA
Go ahead.

VICTORIA
When Mara appeared at the door of this family after your accident, I was in the middle of negotiating two acquisitions that would have collapsed if the engagement collapsed. I told myself she was you, and that it was a kindness to my son to let him have his fiancée back, however the universe had arranged it. By the time I let myself notice — by the time the scar at her jaw caught me one night at dinner, eight months in — I was already six lies deep. Each lie was easier than the one before. I am sorry. That is not the whole answer. It is the part that fits in a sentence.

LENA
What's the rest of the answer.

VICTORIA
The rest of the answer is in front of you on this desk.

## S#4. Victoria's office. Continuous.

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Victoria slides the envelope across the desk. Cream paper, old-looking, the kind of envelope that has been somewhere dry and dark for a long time. The handwriting on the front — *Elena Cross, in confidence* — is in a hand Lena recognizes before she registers recognizing it. Her mother's hand.

Lena does not pick it up immediately.

[KEY CAMERA]
- ECU: the envelope sliding across oak, the handwriting visible
- CU: Lena's eyes on the writing, recognition arriving
- OTS Lena: Victoria's face — the controlled mask thinning at the edges
- INSERT: the wax seal on the back of the envelope, intact

[DIALOGUE]
LENA
That's my mother's writing.

VICTORIA
Your mother brought it to me three months before she died. She asked me to keep it. She asked me not to open it. She gave me instructions for the only condition under which it should be given to you.

LENA
What condition.

VICTORIA
That a woman came forward claiming to be you. Or — and this is where her wording was specific — that a woman came forward claiming a place at the table that had not been hers to claim. She said *Mara* may come forward. She used that name. Lena. I knew the name *Mara* before you did. I knew it for sixteen years and I never asked your mother who Mara was. I never asked because I assumed it was old grief I didn't have a right to. That is the rest of the answer.

LENA
You let Mara onto this family's stage knowing that name had been in a sealed envelope on your desk for sixteen years.

VICTORIA
I did not connect them until the engagement reception. The moment I saw the documents on the screen behind her, I went into my safe and I took this out. I have been carrying it for ten days. I am giving it to you now because I am out of reasons to keep it.

Lena picks up the envelope. Turns it over. The wax seal is unbroken. Her mother's small monogram pressed into it.

[INSERT/CUTAWAY]
ECU: Lena's thumb on the wax seal, not yet breaking it.

[VO]
LENA (V.O.)
I wanted to break the seal in front of her. I wanted to make her watch me read whatever was inside. Then I thought about my mother, and I knew she had not addressed it to *us.* She had addressed it to me.

[END HOOK]
LENA
(standing, envelope in her hand)
I am going to read this somewhere else. When I have, you and I are going to have one more conversation. After that, whether we have any other conversations will depend on what's in this envelope.

VICTORIA
Thank you for that.

LENA
I'll see myself out.

She does. Victoria stays at her desk, hands flat where the envelope used to be, looking at the empty space.

Hard Cut.

# SHE STOLE MY FACE — EP16

## S#1. Noah's Apartment — Living Room · Dusk

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Noah's apartment — a converted brick loft above his investigator office, two floors up from the street, low warm lamps, the city going amber outside the long windows. The cream envelope from Victoria's office sits in the center of the coffee table, wax seal unbroken, her mother's monogram pressed into it. Lena is alone in the room. Charcoal cashmere turtleneck, dark trousers, mother's necklace at her throat. She is sitting on the edge of the sofa, hands folded in her lap, not touching the envelope.

[KEY CAMERA]
- Wide of the living room, the envelope centered on the coffee table
- MCU Lena seated, hands in her lap
- ECU her thumb passing across the back of her left hand once, slow
- INSERT — the wax seal, intact, the small Sterling monogram

[VO]
LENA (V.O.)
The envelope has been on three different tables tonight. Victoria's desk. My car seat. This coffee table. I keep moving it like that's the same as opening it.

She stands. She does not pick the envelope up. She crosses the room toward the balcony doors.

## S#2. Noah's Apartment — Balcony · Dusk

[VISUAL/ACTION]
The balcony — a narrow steel terrace looking down on a side street, the city stretched dark behind it. Noah is already out there, jacket off, sleeves rolled to the elbow, both forearms resting flat on the iron railing. He doesn't turn when she steps out. He has been waiting for her to come find him on her own time. Lena pulls the glass door behind her. The street noise softens by half.

[KEY CAMERA]
- Wide of the balcony, the two of them at the rail, the city low and dim behind
- MCU Noah's forearms on the iron railing — the weight of him grounding it
- OTS Lena to Noah's profile, his eyes on the middle distance
- ECU her hand resting near his on the rail, not touching

[DIALOGUE]
LENA
I want to tell you something I haven't told anyone. It's not about the letter.

NOAH
Take whatever order you want with it. I'm not on a clock.

A beat. She watches the street, not him.

LENA
The night Ethan dragged me off the steps of the engagement reception, I went back to my apartment and I tried to figure out what I was feeling. I expected to feel his loss. I had been planning to marry him in seven months. I had picked the food, the music, the line at the end of my own vows.

NOAH
And.

LENA
And what I felt wasn't him. It was the version of me who had been planning to be happy. She was gone. She had been gone since I woke up in that hospital and a nurse asked me what year it was. I have been afraid of her ever since. Of who I am without her.

Noah does not look away from the middle distance. He gives her the silence for as long as she needs it.

[DIALOGUE]
LENA (CONT'D)
I have been afraid that if I get my life back, I'll find out that woman is still gone and I'm just wearing her clothes. I have been carrying that around for fourteen months and I have not said it out loud until right now.

NOAH
Thank you for saying it to me.

LENA
That's all you're going to say.

NOAH
No. I'm just trying to say the rest of it without being clever about it.

## S#3. Noah's Apartment — Balcony · Continuous

[VISUAL/ACTION]
He turns toward her, finally. Both forearms still on the rail. He doesn't move closer. He just turns his face.

[KEY CAMERA]
- MCU Noah, the city lights catching the slight squint at his eye
- OTS Noah to Lena, her hands now resting on the rail beside his
- ECU Lena's hand near his, not yet touching
- CU Noah, speaking without theater

[DIALOGUE]
NOAH
I stopped working for Cross Industries the day I saw Mara's wrist in the crash footage. I called Victoria's chief of staff that afternoon and I told him I was off the retainer. I haven't taken a new case in two weeks. I didn't tell you that part because I didn't want you thinking I was here on a clock. I'm not. I'm here because of you, not for you. There's a difference, and I want you to hear it.

LENA
Say it without hedging.

A beat. He looks at her directly. No deflection.

NOAH
I love you. That's the only line I have.

LENA
That makes two of us.

[KEY CAMERA]
- ECU Lena's eyes on his — not glassy, steady
- ECU Noah's hand, the slight uncurl of his fingers on the rail
- OTS Lena to Noah's mouth, just after the line lands

## S#4. Noah's Apartment — Balcony · Continuous

[VISUAL/ACTION]
She reaches first. Her hand goes from the rail to the front of his shirt, the heel of her palm against his sternum. He does not move toward her until she does. His forearm weight comes off the railing slowly. His right hand finds the side of her jaw. His thumb rests against the line of her cheekbone. He waits there for one breath. She closes the distance.

They kiss. Slow. Open mouth. His mouth gives her the second she wants to pull back, and she does not pull back.

[KEY CAMERA]
- OTS Noah → Lena CU
- ECU breath stop — Lena's eyes closing
- ECU lips meeting
- CU Lena's hand on his lapel, fingers closing on the fabric
- MCU pull-back two-shot — both of them at the rail, mouths still close
- Wide of the balcony, the city behind, holding

[VISUAL/ACTION]
They break apart. Not fast. His forehead rests against hers a second longer. His thumb is still against her cheekbone. Her hand is still on his shirt. Neither of them apologizes for any of it.

[DIALOGUE]
LENA
I'm going to read the letter tonight. I want you in the room when I do. Not in this room. Just in the apartment.

NOAH
I'll be in the apartment.

She nods, once. Her hand drops from his shirt. She steps back. She looks at the envelope through the glass, still on the coffee table, the wax seal intact.

[VO]
LENA (V.O.)
I had been afraid of who I would be if I got my life back. I hadn't expected to get someone else with it.

## S#5. Across the Street — Lit Window in the Building Opposite · Continuous

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Cut hard across the side street. A floor and a half above Noah's balcony, in a corner apartment in the building opposite, a single floor lamp burns. A woman stands at the window — Mara, hair severely back, charcoal blouse, surgical scar at her neck unhidden by the angle. She has been holding a pair of compact binoculars to her eyes. She lowers them slowly. The kiss has already happened.

A man's silhouette is behind her — taller, older, half a step out of the light. We do not see his face. A hand settles on her shoulder. The hand does not squeeze. It just rests there.

[KEY CAMERA]
- Wide of the apartment opposite, the single floor lamp glowing
- MCU Mara at the window, binoculars in her hand at her side
- ECU the older hand on her shoulder, signet ring catching the lamplight
- OTS Mara to Noah's balcony in the distance — Lena and Noah still at the rail

[DIALOGUE]
VOICE (low, behind her)
Your sister is ready for her real introduction now.

MARA
She's not my sister.

VOICE
She will be by the time I'm finished with this.

[END HOOK]
Mara raises the binoculars again. Through her glass — ECU Lena turning back into the apartment, the envelope still on the coffee table. The hand on Mara's shoulder lifts. The man steps back into the dark behind her without a sound.

[GRAPHIC/UI]
LOWER THIRD — black, white text:
**SHE SAW THEM KISS. SHE HAS PLANS.**
**SHE STOLE MY FACE. EP 17.**

Hard Cut.

# SHE STOLE MY FACE — EP17

## S#1. Sterling Holdings — Lobby · Morning

[VISUAL/ACTION]
The Sterling Holdings tower — black glass, brass letters above the security desk. LENA STERLING steps through the revolving door for the first time in eight months. Hair half-up with a single pearl pin, midnight-blue silk evening dress traded for a slate wool day suit, mother's gold-diamond necklace back at her throat. The receptionist stands. Two of the building's senior partners — older men in grey suits — wait at the elevator bank with leather portfolios.

[KEY CAMERA]
- Wide of the lobby as Lena crosses the marble
- MCU two-shot Lena and the receptionist
- ECU Lena's hand on the necklace pendant, settling it once
- OTS over the partners' shoulders as the elevator opens

[DIALOGUE]
RECEPTIONIST
Welcome back, Ms. Sterling. Your office has been kept exactly as you left it. The lawyers are on twenty-six whenever you're ready.

LENA
Thank you, Iris. Did anyone come in this morning before me?

RECEPTIONIST
Mr. Cross is in the visitor lounge on twenty-two. He's been waiting since seven.

LENA
Of course he has. Tell the lawyers I'll be twenty minutes late.

She walks past the partners with a small nod. They step aside without being asked.

## S#2. Sterling Holdings — 22nd Floor Visitor Lounge · Morning

[VISUAL/ACTION]
A glass-walled lounge looking down on the atrium. ETHAN CROSS sits on the edge of a low couch, suit jacket folded once over his knee. He stands when she enters. In his right hand a small velvet box. He's been holding it long enough that the velvet has gone warm.

[KEY CAMERA]
- MCU Ethan rising
- ECU the velvet box in his hand
- Two-shot Lena stopping three steps inside the door
- CU Lena's face — composed, not cold

[DIALOGUE]
ETHAN
You came down. I wasn't sure you would.

LENA
I told the desk to send you up if you came. Sit down, Ethan. You don't have to stand for me.

ETHAN
I'd like to. For a minute.

He doesn't open the box, but he lifts it once so she sees it.

ETHAN (CONT'D)
I came to ask you something properly. The way I should have done it the first time. My family has already drafted a statement. We can hold a press call tomorrow morning, re-announce the engagement before noon. The original date in the spring. Nothing else changes.

LENA
Ethan. Put that down.

He sets the box on the side table between them. She sits across from him.

LENA (CONT'D)
I appreciate that you came in person. I really do. But I'm going to tell you what I should have said in the hospital, if I'd been clear enough to say anything at all.

ETHAN
Tell me.

LENA
I'm not yours anymore. I haven't been since the morning you stood in front of the cathedral doors and didn't recognize my voice. I know your mother told the guards to remove me. I know you didn't give that order. But I watched your face while it happened, and you didn't look for me. You looked at her. So that's the moment. Not the engagement party, not the hospital — that morning at the doors. That's when it ended.

[KEY CAMERA]
- CU Ethan's jaw, the muscle along it tightening once
- ECU his hand flattening on the box, then sliding off

ETHAN
I have spent every week since trying to apologize for that morning.

LENA
I know you have. And I want you to keep going. Not with me. With your life. Do good things with the resources you have. Be careful about who's standing next to you when the cameras come on. That's the only thing I want from you.

ETHAN
Is there someone? Noah, I'm guessing.

LENA
Yes. He's the first person who acted like he understood what he had.

He nods. Once. He picks the velvet box up, slides it back into his jacket pocket.

ETHAN
Then I won't waste your morning. Thank you for seeing me, Lena. I mean that.

LENA
Take care of yourself, Ethan.

He leaves first. She watches the door close behind him and stays in the chair another full minute before she stands.

## S#3. Sterling Holdings — Lena's Office · Late Morning

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Lena's corner office — restored, dust covers pulled off the credenza, fresh flowers on the desk. NOAH KEENE waits by the window, midnight-blue investigator coat open, camera bag at his feet. He turns when she comes in.

[KEY CAMERA]
- Two-shot at the window
- ECU Lena's reflection beside Noah's in the glass
- MCU pull back to the room

[DIALOGUE]
NOAH
The lawyers said you postponed. I thought I'd wait. How did it go.

LENA
He brought a ring. He had a re-engagement plan with talking points. He left with the ring in his pocket. He was decent about it.

NOAH
That's more than I'd have given him.

LENA
I gave him exactly what I owed him. Nothing extra.

She steps up to the window beside him. Her shoulder brushes his coat sleeve and stays there. Down on the access road, Ethan's black sedan pulls away from the loading bay and merges into traffic.

NOAH
You okay.

LENA
Eight months ago I was certain I was going to marry that man. I just watched him drive away and I felt nothing close to regret. I'm trying to decide if that's strength or if I've just gone numb.

NOAH
You're not numb — you were paying attention. That's different.

[VO]
LENA (V.O.)
He told me once that he sees the moment people stop pretending. I think that's what I just did with Ethan. I stopped pretending I owed him a longer goodbye than he gave me at the cathedral.

## S#4. Sterling Holdings — Lena's Office · Late Morning (continuous)

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Lena's phone buzzes on the desk behind them. She turns. Picks it up. Encrypted message thread, sender field empty. One line of text.

[KEY CAMERA]
- ECU phone screen
- CU Lena's face going very still
- OTS Noah reading over her shoulder

[GRAPHIC/UI]
UNKNOWN: Halcyon doesn't forget. Your mother knew that.

[DIALOGUE]
NOAH
Halcyon Trust. Your mother set it up before you were born.

LENA
That's a name I haven't heard since I was a child. My mother used to take meetings with someone she called the Halcyon people. I assumed it was a charitable trust. She wouldn't talk about it.

NOAH
That phrasing — your mother knew that. Someone's putting you on notice. Don't answer it. Let me trace it first.

LENA
Noah. Look at me.

He does.

LENA (CONT'D)
Whatever this is, we go straight at it. I'm done being the one who finds out last.

[INSERT/CUTAWAY]

[END HOOK]

# SHE STOLE MY FACE — EP19

## S#1. Cross Manor — Private Library · Afternoon

[VISUAL/ACTION]
The Cross Manor library — dark wood, two stories, a tall ladder on rails. VICTORIA CROSS sits in a wingback near the unlit fireplace, a glass of water untouched beside her. LENA STERLING enters without being announced. She doesn't sit. The mother's necklace at her throat catches the slant of afternoon light.

[KEY CAMERA]
- Wide of the library, Lena entering from the long side
- MCU Victoria looking up over reading glasses
- ECU Victoria's hand setting the glasses on the chair arm
- Two-shot as Lena stops at the end of the rug

[DIALOGUE]
VICTORIA
I assumed you'd come. I didn't think it would be today.

LENA
I asked Iris not to call ahead. I wanted to see your face when I came through that door, not the one you put on after a phone call.

VICTORIA
Sit, Lena. Please.

LENA
I won't be long enough to sit. I have one question for you. I'd like a long answer. I'm not interested in a clean one.

VICTORIA
Then ask.

LENA
Eight months ago, before the engagement was even announced, your office paid Noah Keene to surveil me. He told me last week. He didn't tell me sooner because he was still working out who'd hired the firm above his. The chain ends at Cross Industries' general counsel, and the request line came out of your private staff. So I want to know why a woman who was about to become my mother-in-law was paying a private investigator to watch me before there was anything to watch.

[KEY CAMERA]
- CU Victoria's face — the muscles around her mouth not moving
- ECU her thumb pressing once, hard, into her palm
- MCU Lena waiting

VICTORIA
You're entitled to the long answer. Sit down. I won't insult you with one that fits on an index card.

She gestures at the chair opposite. Lena considers, then takes it. Across from each other, not next to.

## S#2. Cross Manor — Private Library · Afternoon (continuous)

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Victoria reaches to the side table, opens a small inlaid box, removes nothing. Closes it again. A gesture of habit.

[KEY CAMERA]
- ECU the inlaid box opening and closing
- MCU Victoria in the wingback
- OTS Lena watching

[DIALOGUE]
VICTORIA
Your mother and I were not close, but we kept up. Twice a year, lunch at the Madison. The last lunch we had — you would have been about seventeen — she stayed at the table after the plates were cleared. She wasn't a woman who lingered, so I noticed. She told me that there was someone out there who would one day come for you. She didn't say a name, she didn't say a connection. She said it like she'd already worked out that I would be in the room when it happened.

LENA
And you assumed what.

VICTORIA
I assumed a stalker. A fixated employee, someone she'd dismissed, an old admirer. Helena was a beautiful woman who said no to a great many men. The list of candidates I drew up for her in my head was not short. I told her I'd watch the perimeter when the time came. I thought I knew what perimeter meant.

LENA
And what did she say back to you.

VICTORIA
She said — and these were her exact words, Lena, I have not changed them in fourteen years — she said: "Victoria. It will be someone with my face."

[KEY CAMERA]
- ECU Lena's hand finding the necklace
- CU Lena's face — the small involuntary swallow
- ECU Victoria's eyes holding steady

VICTORIA (CONT'D)
I asked her if she meant a relative. She said it wasn't the kind of relative she could explain to me over coffee. I let it sit. She paid for lunch. We never had that conversation again. She was diagnosed eleven months later and gone in another fourteen.

LENA
So when Ethan brought me home, you remembered that lunch.

VICTORIA
I remembered every word of it. I hired the firm three days after he told me he was going to propose. I told myself I was protecting the family from a scandal. I was telling myself a more comfortable version of what I was actually afraid of.

LENA
Which was.

VICTORIA
That your mother had been right, and that I had let fourteen years go by without doing one useful thing about it.

## S#3. Cross Manor — Private Library · Afternoon (continuous)

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Lena does not stand. She sets her hand flat on the arm of the chair and leaves it there. Victoria, for the first time in the conversation, looks tired.

[KEY CAMERA]
- Two-shot from a low angle, the fireplace dark between them
- ECU Lena's hand on the chair arm, knuckles paled by pressure
- CU Victoria's face — the corner of her mouth pulling in once

[DIALOGUE]
LENA
Did you ever look into who that person might be. In all those years. Did you ever once put a name to her warning.

VICTORIA
I'm going to give you the answer you already know. I'm sorry, Lena. I let it slide. After she died I told myself she'd been sick longer than the diagnosis. The lunch had been the first sign, and I made her the unreliable narrator of her own life so I wouldn't have to do the work she asked me to do. That is the truth, and I am ashamed of it, and I'm telling you in this room because I owe you the conversation I did not give her.

LENA
Mara walked into my engagement and you stood in front of her at the cathedral doors and told the guards to remove me.

VICTORIA
I did.

LENA
You looked at her face, which is my face, and you did not stop.

VICTORIA
I did not stop. I have walked back through that morning every night since the cathedral, and I want you to know that. I'm not asking you to forgive it — I'm telling you I sat with it.

LENA
Did your husband know about the lunch. About what my mother said.

VICTORIA
Charles was abroad that month. I never brought it up afterward. It was the kind of thing one filed away. I filed it away.

[VO]
LENA (V.O.)
Mom warned a woman who didn't want to be warned. I'm sitting in front of that woman now, and I can see for the first time that she's not the villain of this. She's the witness who looked the other way. Those are not the same thing. They are also not very different.

## S#4. Cross Manor — Drive · Late Afternoon

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Lena outside in the gravel drive. Her car waiting. NOAH KEENE leans against the passenger door, arms crossed, sunglasses pushed up into his hair. He pushes off as she approaches.

[KEY CAMERA]
- Wide of the manor behind her
- MCU two-shot at the car
- ECU Lena's hand opening the door, then closing it, then leaning her forearm on the roof
- CU her face — composed but the eyes wet at the corners

[DIALOGUE]
NOAH
That long inside, huh.

LENA
She told me what my mother had told her at a lunch I wasn't at, fourteen years ago. My mother had said someone was going to come for me — someone with her face. Victoria thought it meant a stalker. It didn't.

NOAH
So she knew the whole time.

LENA
She knew enough to be warned. She didn't know enough to act. That's a different kind of failure than the one I came here to accuse her of, and I don't know what to do with it yet.

NOAH
You don't have to do anything with it today. You sit with it. We keep working.

LENA
The man in the lobby photo.

NOAH
I have a name. Probable. I want one more confirmation before I bring it to you. Get in the car. I'll drive.

She nods. Gets in. The manor's front door, behind them, stays open. Victoria stands in the doorway. She doesn't lift a hand.

[INTERCUT]
- Lena's car pulling out of the drive
- Victoria still in the doorway, watching the car go, then turning back into the dark of the hall

[END HOOK]
INSERT — Lena's phone on the dashboard. A new message, unknown sender.

[GRAPHIC/UI]
UNKNOWN: She told you about the lunch. She didn't tell you what your mother did the year before.

Lena reads it. Sets the phone face-down. Looks straight ahead through the windshield. Noah, driving, glances over.

NOAH (CONT'D)
Same number.

LENA
Then drive faster.

- Lena alone in her apartment. The sealed envelope from her mother on the desk under the glass paperweight.
- ECU her fingertips lifting the paperweight off. Setting it aside.
- ECU her thumb resting on the wax seal — not breaking it. Just touching.
- CU her face — she has been sitting like this since Noah left.

A hospital room, fourteen months ago. Lena, hair still long, eyes half-lidded under sedation. A nurse leaning over with a chart. From Lena's blurred POV — a second figure at the foot of the bed, a woman with her own face, smiling once and turning away before the focus clears. The image dissolves.

[VO]
LENA (V.O.)
I told the doctors I'd hallucinated her. They wrote it down as a sedation artifact. Tonight I'm not so sure anymore.

## S#3. Noah's Car — Parking Garage · Late Night

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Noah's car in the deserted garage. Engine off. Dome light on. The folder of photographs spread across the passenger seat. He calls Lena on speaker.

[KEY CAMERA]

- ECU the phone screen — call connected, LENA
- OTS the photographs on the seat

LENA
You got it.

I got most of it. The name is redacted on the settlor file. I'll have to find another way at the unredacted copy. But the date of birth is on the page, and it was corrected by hand once, so a person was sitting at that desk in 1995 making sure the number was right. December 17, 1995.

LENA
Three months before me.

NOAH
Three months before you. Your mother set up an irrevocable trust seven months into her pregnancy with you, naming another beneficiary born three months earlier. That's not a contingency plan, Lena. That's an existing child.

She doesn't answer immediately. He hears her sit down. The chair leg drags on the floor.

That's the same age as Mara.

Same age window. I haven't matched the birthday yet. I'm bringing the photographs now. I can be at your door in thirty minutes.

Yes. Come.

She ends the call. Stays in the chair. The letter from her mother sits at her elbow under a glass paperweight. She does not pick it up.

## S#4. Lena's Apartment — Living Room · Late Night

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Lena's apartment, low lamp light. The photographs spread on the coffee table. Lena sits on the rug in front of the couch, knees drawn up under her, mother's necklace warm against the hollow of her throat. NOAH sits on the couch behind her, one hand on the back of her neck, not moving — just resting.

- MCU Lena from the front, the photos in her lap
- CU Noah's hand at her nape, thumb still
- ECU the date in blue ink on the corrected document

[DIALOGUE]
LENA
She set this up when I was a heartbeat inside her — time to think about the wording, choose a trustee, redact the name before any of us could see it. That wasn't a panic. That was a plan.

NOAH
It was a plan.

LENA
A plan to provide for someone she could not name out loud to my father. A plan that she expected to outlive her, since she made it irrevocable. A plan she did not tell me about even when she was dying.

NOAH
You think she meant to.

LENA
The letter. The one on the desk. I think she meant to.

She turns her head once toward the desk. Doesn't get up. Noah's hand at her nape settles a degree warmer.

NOAH
You don't have to open it tonight. You don't have to open it tomorrow either. We have a date of birth and a redacted name. That's enough work for tonight.

LENA
The man in the lobby photo.

NOAH
I'm running the cheekbone against the trust company's known associates and against your mother's old social register. I'll have a probable name by morning.

[VO]
LENA (V.O.)
Mom set up a trust three months before I was born. She named a child who already existed. She paid a company called Halcyon to hold the file for thirty years and counting. I am furious with her for not telling me, and I miss her so much it crowds my chest. Both of those are true. I do not know how to put them down.

[VISUAL/ACTION]

- CU Lena noticing

Noah.

NOAH
I see it.

Since I came up. He's not hiding. He wants you to see him.

LENA

NOAH
Same plates. Or the same lack of them.

He doesn't move his hand from her nape. She doesn't move her head from his knee. The curb car sits.

[END HOOK]
The lobby photograph face-up on the table — the man's partial profile catching the lamp light. Slow push in on the ear, the cheekbone, the line of the jaw. Lena's voice, very quiet.

LENA (CONT'D)

## S#2. Lena's Apartment — Kitchen · Morning (continuous)

LENA
"Tell me how much you want this kept private. We can discuss numbers. Or I publish what your mother actually did." Sign-off — M, period.

Lena.

NOAH
All right.

## S#3. Lena's Apartment — Living Room · Morning

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Lena on her couch, phone on the coffee table on speaker. The photograph and the typewritten note bagged in a Ziploc beside it. ANDREA QUINN — Lena's family PR head, late forties, dressed for a hospital visit she's running between — answers on the second ring.

[DIALOGUE]
ANDREA

LENA
I'm going to be brief. I need a press conference set up for tomorrow morning. Ten a.m. Sterling Holdings rooftop terrace. Twelve seats. No more. The list is yours, but I want the Times, the Journal, AP, and Andrea — I want at least three independents. No tabloid frontline. They get the wire feed.

The topic is my mother. Specifically — a sealed letter she left me that I have not opened. I'm going to announce I'm opening it on my own terms, and I'm going to announce that there is a woman who has been impersonating me and who has now contacted me directly with a financial threat tied to my mother's private papers. I am going to name her. I am going to say I will deal with her in public, on my schedule, and not hers.

You are going to name her.

Mara Voss. Out loud. To the cameras.

ANDREA
The legal team will want a sit-down before that podium.

The legal team will get one this afternoon. I'll be at the office by noon. Set the room for two o'clock. Mr. Henderson, Ms. Patel, the litigation partner whose name I never remember, and whoever else they want. Get me a draft statement by five.

ANDREA
Five-thirty. I have a procedure waiting.

LENA
Five-thirty. Thank you, Andrea. Go in.

She ends the call. Sits with both hands flat on her thighs. Across the room, the front door buzzer goes. She rises and goes to let Noah in.

## S#4. Lena's Apartment — Living Room · Afternoon

[VISUAL/ACTION]

I'm doing neither.

NOAH
You're doing the third thing. Going public.

LENA
The press conference at ten tomorrow. I'm telling the room I have a letter from my mother. I haven't opened it yet. And there is a woman trying to monetize whatever's in it — I'm naming her. After that, every move she makes is in front of cameras.

NOAH
You realize the moment you say the letter exists out loud, someone will try to take it.

LENA
The letter goes in a Sterling Holdings vault tonight. Two-person check-in. The only people with the code are me, Andrea, and the building's chief of security. No one else, including you.

NOAH
Good.

LENA
You're not insulted.

NOAH
I'm relieved. Don't put me on the list. Put me on a different list — the list of people who can shoot a hand off a doorframe at twenty feet if anyone tries the long way around. You can have my name twice on that one.

[VO]
LENA (V.O.)
Mom left me a letter and a question and a girl with my face. I'm going to answer all three of them in the order I choose. Mara wants a private negotiation. She is going to get the most public conversation of either of our lives.

## S#5. Lena's Apartment — Living Room · Afternoon (continuous)

From this evening forward, no one — no executor, no family lawyer, no Cross Industries staff, no foundation employee — has solo access to anything of my mother's. Foundation office included.

## S#1. Sterling Holdings — Rooftop Terrace · Morning

[VISUAL/ACTION]
A rooftop terrace forty stories up — a clear morning, the city laid flat behind glass partitions. Twelve chairs in three rows of four, a low podium with a single microphone, the Sterling family monogram on a discreet plaque. A small bank of press: a Times reporter, a Journal reporter, an AP correspondent, three independents, two staffers from the trade press, three more cleared seats. LENA STERLING in the wing, charcoal pencil skirt and navy silk blouse, mother's necklace at her throat, no other jewelry. ANDREA QUINN at her shoulder with a single index card.

- MCU Lena in the wing
- ECU her thumb on the index card
- OTS Andrea murmuring close to her ear

[DIALOGUE]

The legal team wants you to keep her surname out of it. I'm telling them you won't. I'm telling them you'll name her three times in two minutes and they should make their peace with it before the camera turns on.

LENA
Tell them I'll name her four times.

ANDREA
You will not.

LENA
Three. I'll keep it at three.

ANDREA
Take a breath. Go when the red light is on.

The red light on the camera in front of the podium flicks on.

## S#2. Sterling Holdings — Rooftop Terrace · Morning (continuous)

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Lena walks to the podium. No prepared sheet on the surface — only the index card she does not put down. She rests one hand lightly on the wood, the other at her side.

[KEY CAMERA]
- MCU Lena at the podium
- OTS over the press row, the city behind
- CU her face, calm, the necklace catching morning light
- Intercut occasional reaction shots — Times reporter writing, AP correspondent's recorder light

[DIALOGUE]
LENA
Good morning. Thank you for coming on short notice. I'll be brief and I'll take questions for ten minutes after.

She pauses. Lets the room settle.

LENA (CONT'D)
Most of you covered my engagement and the cathedral last spring. Most of you covered the correction of the public record at the Cross family announcement in October. I'm not here today to revisit either of those events. I'm here because of my mother.

ECU — her thumb finds the edge of the necklace once, returns to the podium.

LENA (CONT'D)
Helena Sterling died eight years ago next March. Before she died, she wrote me a letter. I have not opened it. It has been in my family's papers since the week after her funeral. I will tell you, plainly, why I have not opened it — because I assumed for eight years that whatever it contained was meant to bring me peace, and I was not in a hurry for that kind of peace. I am opening it this week, on my own terms. I'm telling you about it now because I've been given a reason to.

She lifts the index card once. Sets it back down without reading from it.

LENA (CONT'D)
For the last several weeks, a woman who is not me has been representing herself as me to private actors in this city and abroad. Some of you covered the original incident at the cathedral when she did the same in public. Her name is Mara Voss. She is the same woman who stood at that altar last spring under my legal name. She has, in the last forty-eight hours, contacted me personally in writing, with a financial demand, attached to a claim that my mother's private papers contain information she intends to monetize.

A small wave through the press row. Three pens moving faster. The Times reporter looks up.

LENA (CONT'D)
I'm here to say two things to her in front of all of you, since she has been clear she prefers the leverage of privacy. First — I will not be paying her. Not today, not next week, not in any month. Second — there is a question about my family's history that I intend to answer, and I intend to answer it in my mother's voice, not in hers. I am opening the letter this week. Whatever it says, I will share what is appropriate to share. There is a woman who believes she has a right to my family name. We will deal with that in due course, in public, and on my schedule, not hers.

She lets the moment hold.

LENA (CONT'D)
I will take questions.

## S#3. Sterling Holdings — Rooftop Terrace · Morning (continuous)

LENA
Yes. That is what I'm alleging, on the public record, with this microphone in front of me. The Cross family has been informed of that allegation through their counsel as of yesterday afternoon. I have no further comment on Cross family matters from this podium.

JOURNAL REPORTER
You said she contacted you with a financial demand. Are you in possession of evidence of that contact.

LENA
I am. It was hand-delivered yesterday morning. It is in the custody of my private security and has been logged with my legal team. I will not be reading it aloud today. I will note that it includes a photograph from my mother's personal collection, which raises its own questions about access to my family's records. Those questions will be answered separately.

AP CORRESPONDENT

LENA
That depends on what's in it. If it's a private letter, it remains private. If it contains information that bears on Ms. Voss's claims, I will return to this podium. I will not be ambushed into a tabloid timeline. If a leak appears before I'm ready, you will know it was not mine.

INDEPENDENT REPORTER

LENA
He is not in this room. He's not part of this conversation. He's part of my private life, which is something Ms. Voss has also tried to weaponize this week. I'd ask you not to do the same favor for her. Next question.

[VO]
LENA (V.O.)
She wanted me to text a number — she got a podium instead. She wanted my mother's letter as her leverage; I just made it my schedule. That is the work for today.

- ECU eyes locked, the second of his patience
- CU his hand on her jaw — not her thigh, not her ribs, her jaw
- ECU kiss — lips meeting, slow, open mouth
- MCU sit-down hold, her back against the counter, his forearm braced beside her on the stone

[VISUAL/ACTION]
The kiss does not escalate. He holds her there at the counter, one forearm flat on the stone beside her, the other hand still at her jaw. She is the one who pulls back first — not fast, not far. Her forehead rests against his collarbone. His hand stays at her jaw. He does not move it.

[DIALOGUE]
NOAH
The pan's going to scorch.

LENA
Move the pan.

He reaches blindly behind himself, slides the pan off the heat with the back of his wrist. He does not let go of her jaw to do it. She laughs, once — small, real, the first time she has laughed in three days.

## S#4. Lena's Apartment — Living Room Floor · Later

[VISUAL/ACTION]
They are sitting on the floor with their backs against the sofa, plates in their laps, the lamp low. Two glasses of water on the rug between them. The food is on the plates, not on the table. Neither of them has commented on it. Outside the long windows, the city has gone fully dark.

[KEY CAMERA]
- Wide of the living room, the two of them on the floor
- MCU side-on, plates in their laps
- ECU Lena's hand reaching across to take a piece of bread off his plate
- Two-shot, both of them eating slow

[DIALOGUE]
LENA
You can't cook like that and live alone.

NOAH
I do though — and I know that's not what you said.

A beat. She takes another piece of bread. Doesn't ask permission.

LENA
Were you a cook before you were an investigator.

NOAH
I was a Marine before I was a cook. The cooking is from the Marine part, actually.

LENA
I'm not going to ask about the Marine part tonight.

NOAH
I wasn't going to volunteer it.

They eat in quiet for a long beat. Comfortable. She leans her shoulder against his shoulder. He does not adjust to take her weight; he just lets it land.

[VO]
LENA (V.O.)
I had forgotten that you are allowed to eat dinner on the floor.

## S#5. Lena's Apartment — Living Room · Continuous

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Her phone buzzes on the rug. She picks it up automatically, not really looking. Then she does look. Unknown number. A live video feed, full-screen, no caller ID.

[KEY CAMERA]
- ECU her phone screen — a security cam view of her own building's lobby
- ECU Noah's eyes catching the screen
- INSERT — on the screen, the elevator doors opening. A woman steps out. Charcoal blouse. Hair severely back. Mara.

[VISUAL/ACTION]
On the screen, Mara walks slowly across her own lobby toward the camera. She is alone. She stops in front of the lens. She looks straight into it. She is not smiling. She does not look afraid. She lifts her chin slightly so the camera gets her face plainly.

[DIALOGUE]
MARA (ON SCREEN)
Can we talk in person finally. I'm not here to hurt anyone tonight. I'm at your door in about a minute. You can answer it or you can call your security, but if you call security you will not get the conversation I came to give you. I came alone. I'll be at your door in about a minute.

The feed cuts. The phone goes black.

[END HOOK]
[KEY CAMERA]
- ECU Lena's phone, black
- MCU Lena and Noah on the floor, plates in their laps
- ECU Noah's hand setting his plate down, slow
- OTS Lena to the apartment door, twenty feet away, still shut

[DIALOGUE]
LENA
She knows I'm in here.

NOAH
She knows you're in here with me too.

LENA
Don't leave.

NOAH
Wouldn't dream of it.

The faint mechanical sound of the building's elevator arriving on Lena's floor. The doors opening, soft, off-screen.

[GRAPHIC/UI]
LOWER THIRD — black, white text:
**SHE CAME ALONE. SHE BROUGHT QUESTIONS.**
**SHE STOLE MY FACE. EP 24.**

Hard Cut.

# SHE STOLE MY FACE — EP24

## S#1. Lena's Apartment — Entry Hall · Night

[VISUAL/ACTION]
The faint hum of the elevator settles. A pause in the hallway outside. Lena is already on her feet, crossing to the door. Noah a half-step behind her, not touching her — just there. She does not look through the peephole. She undoes the deadbolt herself. She opens the door before Mara has the chance to knock.

Mara is in the corridor. Hands at her sides, no purse, charcoal blouse the way she wore it on Lena's lobby camera ninety seconds ago. The surgical scar two centimeters under her left ear is not covered tonight. She has not done her hair to hide it. She looks at Lena. She looks past Lena at Noah. She looks back at Lena.

[KEY CAMERA]
- MCU Lena opening the door
- OTS Lena to Mara in the corridor
- ECU Mara's scar at her jaw, unhidden
- Wide of the entry hall — Noah a half-step back, Lena's hand still on the deadbolt

[DIALOGUE]
MARA
Thank you for opening it.

LENA
Come in. Take off your coat. Sit on the couch, not the chair. I want to see your hands.

MARA
That's fair.

She steps inside. She takes off her coat and hangs it herself on the hook beside Noah's camera bag. She walks past them into the living room. She sits on the couch where Lena told her to sit. She puts her hands flat on her knees, palms down. She is not performing it. It is the posture of someone who has been waiting a long time to do exactly this.

[INTERCUT]
- The building's lobby, twenty floors below. An older man on the long bench by the security desk — dark coat, dark trousers, a leather satchel at his side. He has been searched. The two night guards are watching him from the desk. He has not looked at the elevator bank once. His hands are folded in his lap.
- ECU his hand, the knuckles white where one thumb has been pressing the other for some time.

## S#2. Lena's Apartment — Living Room · Continuous

[VISUAL/ACTION]
The plates from earlier are still on the rug. Noah collects them in one quiet motion and carries them to the kitchen. He does not leave the apartment. He stays at the kitchen island, twenty feet away, where he can see and hear. Lena sits in the armchair across from Mara. She crosses her ankles. She waits.

[KEY CAMERA]
- Wide of the living room — Mara on the couch, Lena in the chair, Noah at the island in the background
- MCU Mara, palms flat on her knees
- OTS Mara to Lena, the necklace at Lena's throat catching the lamp
- ECU Mara's eyes — tired, not soft

[DIALOGUE]
MARA
I'm not going to apologize tonight. I want to say that before I start, because I have been told a hundred times in the last three weeks that I should walk in here and grovel. I'm not going to. I don't think you'd believe me if I did, and you would be right not to. I'm here to give you a version of the story you have not heard.

LENA
Go ahead.

MARA
My mother told me from the time I was a kid that I was Helena Sterling's hidden daughter. I'm going to call her my mother in this room, because that's what she has been to me my whole life, and I'm not ready to call her by her name in front of you. She showed me documents at twenty-four. She gave me my real name. She said you had what was supposed to be mine. The man who raised me was not my father. The woman whose name I'd been using on every form was not the woman who gave birth to me. I believed her. I want you to understand I believed her then.

LENA
Go on.

MARA
I went to my mother's side of the story first. I think most people would have. The story she gave me was that Helena Sterling had an affair, she had a child, and she let that child be raised in another house because the affair would have ended her marriage and her access to your father's company. The story I was given was that I had been hidden so that you could exist as the only daughter. Whether any of that is true is a question I am no longer sure of the answer to. I believed it then. I want you to understand that I believed it then.

LENA
Keep going.

MARA
I thought I was taking back my own family name. I thought you would be told the truth and I thought you and I would have to figure out what to do with the family on the other side of it. I did not know I was going to be made into a face. I did not know about Dr. Brandt until I was already in the chair. I thought I had signed up for a smaller procedure — work on my jaw, work on my cheek. I woke up nine hours later. The first time I saw myself in the mirror after the surgery I threw up.

[KEY CAMERA]
- MCU Mara on the couch, not crying — flat, used-up
- ECU Lena's hands in her lap, fingers loose
- OTS Lena to Mara, Noah in the deep background at the island
- CU Mara, the scar at her ear caught at this angle

[DIALOGUE]
MARA (CONT'D)
By the time I understood what I had been turned into, the version of me that had agreed to go this far was someone I could not get back to. The crash on the cliff road happened two weeks after I came out of bandages. I did not drive that car. I did not arrange it either, but I'll tell you who did — not in this sentence, and not without help in this room.

## S#3. Lena's Apartment — Living Room · Continuous

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Lena does not interrupt. She has not interrupted once. She lets Mara finish. She lets the silence sit. When she finally speaks, she speaks low and even.

[KEY CAMERA]
- MCU Lena, the lamp catching half her face
- ECU her hand, finally lifting from her lap, settling on the arm of the chair
- OTS Lena to Mara on the couch
- INSERT — Noah at the island in the background, listening, not moving

[DIALOGUE]
LENA
If everything you just said is true, then you have been used. The person who used you is still using you. I'm not interested in arguing the parts of what you said that I'm not sure I believe. We can get to those later. I want one piece of information from you tonight, and I want it before we go any further. Who told you about Helena being your mother. By name. Not "my mother." Not "the woman who raised me." The name.

A long beat. Mara's hands stay flat on her knees. She looks past Lena at Noah. She looks back at Lena.

MARA
I'll give you the name tonight, but I want one other person in the room when I do. He's downstairs — has been for an hour. He came to your building before I did. If you let me, I'll bring him up.

LENA
Who is he.

MARA
The person who can confirm everything I just said. And a few things I haven't.

Lena looks at Noah. Noah looks at her. He nods once, slow. He lifts his phone — already on the lobby camera feed.

[KEY CAMERA]
- ECU Noah's phone, the lobby feed live — an older man visible alone on a bench
- MCU Lena's face deciding
- OTS Lena to Mara

[DIALOGUE]
LENA
Tell building security he can come up. Tell them I'm expecting him. Tell them I want the elevator security camera live on Noah's phone the whole way.

MARA
He'll be unarmed. He has been searched twice tonight. He let them.

LENA
I'll make my own decision about that when he's in this room.

## S#4. Lena's Apartment — Entry Hall · A Few Minutes Later

[VISUAL/ACTION]
The doorbell. Mara stands. She walks past Lena, asks her quietly with her eyes if she can. Lena nods, once. Mara opens the door herself.

JULIAN VOSS — mid-sixties, gaunt, the kind of thin that comes from travel and from worry. Dark coat, dark trousers, no tie, a leather satchel at his side that has been opened by security tonight. Hair white at the temples and combed back. He carries himself like a man who used to be in better rooms than this. He steps inside. He does not move past the threshold without permission.

[KEY CAMERA]
- MCU Mara opening the door
- OTS Mara to Julian — the lined face, the satchel at his hip
- ECU Julian's eyes finding Lena across the entry hall
- Wide — Julian still at the threshold, Mara stepping aside to let him pass

[DIALOGUE]
MARA
Come in, Julian.

She steps back. She turns to Lena and Noah. The introduction comes out flat and unrehearsed.

MARA (CONT'D)
This is Julian Voss. My real father. He has not seen me in person in nineteen years. He has been in hiding for fourteen of them. The voice you heard on the phone in my apartment after your press conference yesterday morning — that was not him. He has been the one trying to pull me out of the room she's been keeping me in. Julian, this is Lena. This is Noah.

Julian steps fully into the apartment. He sets the satchel down by the door. He looks at Lena. His hands are at his sides. He does not extend one to her. He has the manners not to ask.

[DIALOGUE]
JULIAN
(quietly)
Ms. Sterling. I am sorry for what your mother did not have a chance to tell you.

LENA
You knew my mother.

JULIAN
I knew her for thirty-one years. I knew her well enough that she would not have written that letter in three sealed sections if she had not been planning for the night you and I were standing in the same room. She knew that I would be in a room with you eventually. She did not know it would take this long.

[KEY CAMERA]
- MCU Julian at the entry, the door closing behind him
- OTS Lena to Julian, holding ground
- ECU Lena's thumb against the back of her left hand — the same small motion as the balcony
- CU Noah at the kitchen island, watching Julian like an investigator

[DIALOGUE]
LENA
You said the voice on the phone in Mara's apartment was not you.

JULIAN
No.

LENA
Then who was it.

JULIAN
It was a man who was your mother's friend for fifteen years and her enemy for the last seven. He is the third trustee on a fund called Halcyon. You found the name of that fund last fall in a folder in the bridal room. His name is on the third signature line on a document I am going to give you tonight. He has been using my daughter as a face for that fund's purposes since the day she came out of bandages. I have been trying to get her out of his reach since the week after the crash. I have not been successful. I am here tonight because I am out of moves I can make alone.

LENA
And you believe you can prove all of that.

JULIAN
I have brought what I could carry through three airports. The rest is in a safety deposit box in Lisbon under a name your mother gave me twenty-six years ago. I will give you the name of the box and the name on it before I leave this apartment tonight.

[END HOOK]
[VISUAL/ACTION]
Lena looks at Mara, still standing near the door. She looks at Julian. She looks at Noah at the island. She walks past Julian into the living room. She sits back down in her armchair. She gestures, calmly, to the two ends of the couch.

[KEY CAMERA]
- MCU Lena seated in the armchair
- OTS over Lena's shoulder to Mara and Julian at the door
- Wide of the room — Noah moving from the island to take the chair beside Lena

[DIALOGUE]
LENA
Then sit down. Both of you. Tell me what my mother did not have a chance to tell me.

[GRAPHIC/UI]
LOWER THIRD — black, white text:
**HER FATHER CAME. HER MOTHER LEFT A LETTER.**
**SHE STOLE MY FACE. EP 25.**

# SHE STOLE MY FACE — EP25

## S#1. Sterling Industries — Press Wing · Midday · Two Days Later

[VISUAL/ACTION]
A clean, low-ceilinged press wing at the back of Sterling Industries. Not an atrium. Not a podium. Just three long folding tables along one wall, folders stacked at each — beige clasp envelopes, plain labels, no Sterling crest. A press handler in a charcoal suit hands out one folder per credentialed reporter. Twelve reporters in two loose rows. ANDREA QUINN supervises from the wall, arms folded, not speaking. LENA is not in this room.

[KEY CAMERA]
- Wide of the press wing, the three folding tables
- ECU a folder being handed across — label reads CONSEQUENCE PACKET / 25 OCT / STERLING COMMS
- MCU Andrea at the wall, watching
- Cut to the foyer outside — two small clusters of photographers waiting at separate exits

[INTERCUT]
- Lena, forty stories up, alone at her office window. She is not watching the cameras. She is watching her own reflection in the dark glass.
- ECU her fingertips against the cool window, the city behind her shoulder.

[VO]
LENA (V.O.)
Andrea told me to leave the building before the packets went out. She said I shouldn't be in the same postal code as the cameras today. I stayed anyway. I went up forty floors instead of out. I wanted to see if I would feel anything when the doors opened on the women who had spent a year wearing pieces of my life.

The reporters open the folders. Inside: a single page wire-transfer ledger with TESSA HALE highlighted as the recipient of three payments from a numbered account; a clinic file print-out with CELIA BRANDT, MD on the surgeon line and the same MARA EILEEN VOSS post-op heading the room knows from the engagement-night feed; a copy of a private gambling-debt buyout dated six weeks before the cliff-road crash, also routed through the same numbered account. No editorial commentary. No press release. Just the documents.

[KEY CAMERA]
- ECU the wire transfer ledger, Tessa Hale's name in highlight
- ECU the clinic file header
- ECU the gambling-debt buyout, the dates
- MCU a Times reporter scanning, looking up, scanning again

## S#2. Sterling Industries — Foyer · Twenty Minutes Later

[VISUAL/ACTION]
TESSA HALE — late-20s, auburn waves, camel cardigan over a cream camisole, gold hoops — walks toward the small bank of photographers at the east exit. She has not been arrested. She has not been served. She has been called by her own talent agency at eight this morning and asked to come pick up the rest of her things. She is carrying a small banker's box. She is alone.

She steps into the foyer. The photographers see the box. They start. The flashbulbs come fast. She does not put a hand up. She keeps walking. She has been told by Andrea Quinn personally that putting a hand up will be the photograph that runs.

[KEY CAMERA]
- MCU Tessa walking through the foyer, box held against her hip
- ECU the photographers, the flash array catching her face
- OTS Tessa to the open doors and the bright street
- INSERT — on the box, a curl of cream silk visible, the corner of a friendship-bracelet thread

[VO]
LENA (V.O.)
She had been my friend for nine years. She picked the song at my mother's funeral. She picked it because it was the song my mother sang to her when she stayed with us. Mara handed her a bank statement and a bookie's number and that was enough.

Tessa is past the photographers. She is at the curb. A black town car is waiting — not for her. She does not get into it. She walks. The cameras follow her for fourteen steps before they lose interest. She turns a corner.

## S#3. Sterling Industries — West Exit · Continuous

[VISUAL/ACTION]
At the same time. The west exit, fifty yards over. DR. CELIA BRANDT — early-40s, the caramel bob, the slate blouse under a dark coat. White medical coat folded over her arm. She is carrying her own banker's box. She walks out into the second cluster of photographers. She does not look at the cameras either.

[KEY CAMERA]

- ECU the folded white medical coat, the state license badge clipped to it
- ECU her free hand reaching up — she unclips the license badge as she walks
- INSERT — her hand setting the unclipped badge on top of the folded coat. Voluntary.

Celia walks past the photographers. She is going to her car. The flashbulbs do their work. Nobody stops her. There is no warrant tonight. There is just the next twenty years of her life with this morning in it.

## S#4. Sterling Industries — Lena's Office · Afternoon

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Lena's office at Sterling Industries — heavy oak desk, the long window looking down on the river, mother's necklace at her throat. She is at the window, watching the two corners of the building from forty stories up. From this height she can see the photographers folding tripods at both exits. NOAH knocks softly. He carries two coffees in paper cups. He sets one on her desk.

[KEY CAMERA]
- Wide of the office, Lena at the window
- MCU Noah setting the coffee down
- OTS Lena to the river
- ECU her thumb finding the inscription side of the pendant against her collarbone

[DIALOGUE]
NOAH
Andrea says Tessa is at her cousin's apartment in Brooklyn. The cousin doesn't know yet why she's there. She will by tomorrow.

LENA
Celia.

NOAH
Celia drove to the airport. She's not flying yet. She's just sitting in the long-term lot. My guy out there will tell me when she moves.

LENA
And Mara isn't here today.

NOAH
You made sure of it. Andrea too. So did I.

LENA
Good. I didn't want either of them in the room for this morning — not because I was protecting them. I wanted Tessa and Celia to walk out into nothing — no Mara on a wall to react to. That mattered to me.

NOAH
I know.

LENA
Did Andrea call.

NOAH
The Times will run tomorrow. The Journal already filed. The medical board issued a single paragraph at two. The trades will spend a week on it. There is no formal proceeding from any of this. It will be informal industry consequence — quiet calls, dropped accounts, no contracts at renewal. Andrea says that's the version that lasts.

She turns from the window. She picks up the coffee. She looks at him.

LENA
I want to read the third page tonight.

NOAH
I'll be here.

## S#5. Lena's Office — Desk · After Dark

[VISUAL/ACTION]
The building has gone mostly quiet. Lena sits behind her desk. Lamp on, overhead off. The letter is in front of her — her mother's cream paper, the first two pages unfolded to the right of the desk pad, both faces-down so the writing does not look up at her. The third page is still sealed. The wax over the third section is darker than the wax on the front of the envelope, a different batch. Helena sealed it later, possibly alone. Noah is at the small couch by the door, jacket off, sleeves rolled, reading nothing, just being in the room.

Lena picks up the slim letter knife from the desk caddy. She slides it under the wax of the third seal. The seal lifts cleanly. She unfolds the page.

She does not read it aloud.

[KEY CAMERA]
- ECU the wax seal lifting under the knife
- ECU the page unfolding — handwriting visible but unreadable in this shot
- MCU Lena's face — the lamp catching her
- HOLD on her face. Camera does not pan to the page.
- ECU her eyes moving line by line. One. Two. Three. Then back up to the top of the page.

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Her expression changes once. A small tightening at the mouth. She reads on. It changes a second time — a slow pull at the eyebrow, recognition gathering. She turns the page. She reads the back. It changes a third time. Her hand, which has been holding the page lightly, sets the page flat on the desk and does not move. The final expression is not grief. It is not surprise. It is cold realization. Of something specific. Of something that has now made the rest of the letter make a kind of sense.

[KEY CAMERA]
- ECU Lena's eyes, very still
- ECU her hand flat on the page, fingers spread, no tremor
- MCU pull back — Lena alone behind the desk, Noah on the couch in the background

[VO]
LENA (V.O.)
My mother wrote the third page in October — after the diagnosis, before the radiation. She wrote it for the night a particular person walked into a particular room. For eight years, I'd assumed that person was me.

She turns the page over once more. There is one final line at the bottom of the second side, in the unsteady hand of the last two weeks before her mother's death. Lena's eyes stop on it.

[INSERT/CUTAWAY]
ECU one line in Helena's failing hand, visible to camera for the first time: *Forgive me for the daughter I let her keep.*

She does not read the line aloud.

## S#1. The Envelope · Lena's apartment · early morning

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Lena at the kitchen island. The letter from EP25 is open on the marble in front of her. Her mother's handwriting, slanted, faded. Three pages, one photograph paper-clipped to the back. - CU Lena's hand flattening the paperclip down

My mother had an affair twenty-eight years ago with a man named Julian Voss. She paid him off, paid the woman he was with, and the child they had together — that's who Mara is.

Mara is your mother's mistake. Or your mother's daughter?

LENA
The letter doesn't finish that sentence. That's the part she didn't write down.

Noah crosses behind her, lifts the third page off the marble — gently, two fingers at the corner. He's looking at the back of the page, against the morning light. There's a paragraph there Lena's already read, but there's also a faint line of pencil — almost erased — under the signature.

(rising, taking the page)
That's her shorthand. She used it on case files. Two letters and a year.

What does it say.

(reading, voice flatter)
"J.V. — 1999." Not the year Mara was born. The year before.

A year before. Meaning your mother knew Julian a year before whatever happened with Eileen happened.

Meaning my mother had a reason to want him stayed quiet that didn't have anything to do with a child.

[BEAT]

There's a second story under this letter. She didn't write it down because she didn't want you to find it on the first read.

The paperclipped photograph — a young Helena Sterling, late 20s, in a hotel lobby, half-turned away from the camera. A man's hand at her elbow. The man's face is cut off by the frame.

You. Me. That's it. And it stays there.

Lena folds the letter back into thirds. Slides it into the inside pocket of her coat. Looks at Noah. "Mara needs to think we still don't know."

My mother told me this morning she's been sitting on your mother's paper for four years. I was raised in that house and I never noticed. I'm not here to ask you for anything. I came because I had every reason to recognize you in that event hall and I didn't, and that's mine to live with. I'm sorry. Properly sorry. The kind that doesn't ask anything back.

LENA
(after a long beat)
I accept that. You don't have to carry the part where you didn't recognize me anymore. Go. Live the rest of your life. And be careful what you tell your mother about my next move.

ETHAN
She told me you're going to the lake house tomorrow. She thought I should know in case it goes badly.

LENA
Then you've delivered both messages. Go, Ethan.

He nods once. Doesn't put out his hand. Walks out. The door closes behind him quieter than he came in.

[VO]
LENA (V.O.): For two years I had waited for him to say the small honest thing without dressing it up as a fight to win me back. He had finally said it, and I had less time for it than I'd thought I would.


## S#2. Mara's Apartment — Bathroom · same morning

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Mara at the bathroom sink. Cold water running. She's been splashing it on her face. She straightens, looks at herself in the mirror — surgical scar two centimeters under her left ear catching the overhead light. The face she has been wearing for two years looks back. She lifts her hair off her neck. Sets it down again. Picks up her phone off the counter.

[KEY CAMERA]
- ECU the surgical scar in the mirror
- MCU Mara, half her face wet
- CU her hand picking up the phone

[INTERCUT]
INTERCUT — Lena, in her office, looking at the closed door Ethan just walked out of. Then back to Lena answering a call from a number she does not recognize.

[DIALOGUE]
MARA (V.O., on the line)
Don't say my name. Don't react. Are you alone in your office.

LENA
Yes.

MARA (V.O.)
I went to the cemetery yesterday. I sat at your mother's grave and I asked her to tell me which woman I came from. She didn't answer. So I'm asking you. I don't think I came from her. I don't think I ever did.

LENA
What changed.

MARA (V.O.)
I called my own mother last night to ask about the day I was born. She didn't pick up. Then she called me back two hours later from a number I didn't know, and she told me a story about the hospital that I'd never heard before in twenty-six years. She had to put it together in front of me. I sat on the bathroom floor when we got off the phone. Something is wrong with the woman who raised me, and I don't know what.


## S#3. Mara's Apartment — Bathroom · continuous

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Mara, still at the sink, leans her free hand on the counter. The mirror is fogging slightly from the cold water she ran a minute ago. Her reflection holds, sharper than it should be.

[KEY CAMERA]
- ECU Mara's hand white on the counter
- MCU Mara, phone at her ear, no makeup
- OTS Mara → her own reflection

[DIALOGUE]
LENA
What do you want to do.

MARA (V.O.)
I want to come to the lake house tomorrow the way you asked me to. Just you and me. But I need you to know — if you have something to tell me, I'm not bringing her into the room with me anymore. I'm coming with my own ears on.

LENA
Then come alone.

MARA (V.O.)
I will.

She ends the call. Sets the phone face down on the marble. Looks back at the mirror. The hand near her scar comes up almost on its own and rests there — fingertips flat against the curved white line — as if she is meeting it for the first time.

[VO]
MARA (V.O.)
I had spent two years learning to call a stranger my mother. I had spent twenty-six learning to call a woman in a small apartment mine. I was starting to understand that one of those numbers was wrong. I did not yet know which.


## S#4. Sterling Industries · Lobby · afternoon

[VISUAL/ACTION]
Ground-floor lobby of Sterling Industries. Late afternoon foot traffic — employees crossing the marble toward the revolving door. On the long bench by the elevators, a WOMAN in her late fifties — gray-streaked dark hair pinned tight, a wide-brimmed hat in her lap, a folded coat across her knees. She's been there ten minutes. Calm. She's reading the building directory on her phone.

[KEY CAMERA]
- Wide of the lobby, the woman small at the edge of frame
- MCU the woman, face partially obscured by the hat brim
- CU her hand on the phone — Lena's name highlighted in the directory
- ECU her left thumb where a bone bend across the joint is just visible

[INSERT/CUTAWAY]
A photograph from EP12: Dr. Celia Brandt holding a small framed photo on her clinic shelf — a younger version of the same woman from the bench, an old portrait twenty years ago, slightly turned away. Same thumb bend. Match unmistakable to the audience. Lena does not see it. Ethan, somewhere upstairs, does not know the photograph exists.

[END HOOK]
The woman on the bench presses the elevator call button. The doors open. She steps in. The doors close on her face — calm, unhurried, lipstick fresh. The directory display above her ticks one floor up, then another, then another. She is not stopping at Lena's floor. She is going higher. To a floor Lena's company doesn't own.

Cut to black.

Hard Cut.
